  3. החומר האפור
  4. החומר הלבן
* `category` = `התעלה השדרתית ותכולתה` (matches the requested category exactly), `generation_mode` = `INDEPENDENT`.
* Structural checks: a valid `CandidateQuestion` was returned (pydantic-validated); Hebrew question/answer text present; exactly four answers; `correct_answer` is a valid 1-based position (2); category/mode provenance correct; no `InvalidGeneratedOutputError` was raised, confirming any evidence/historical-reference provenance the model claimed (if any) was consistent with what was actually supplied - `INDEPENDENT` claimed no historical reference, as required.
* Only one live request was made, per the WP's cost/frequency constraint ("do not make repeated live calls trying to improve the question"). Generation success is **not** treated as proof of factual grounding - that remains WP-010's job.

## Live Grounding Smoke Test (WP-010)

**Executed successfully**, once the two prompt fixes described in "Decisions Made (WP-010)" above were applied. One real generate-then-validate pair: `QuestionGenerator.from_default_configuration().generate_candidate_question(category=<first real canonical category>, generation_mode=GenerationMode.INDEPENDENT)` immediately followed by `GroundingValidator.from_default_configuration().validate_grounding(candidate)`.

* Category used: `התעלה השדרתית ותכולתה` (same first canonical category as the WP-009 live test).
* Generated question: `מהו המבנה של התעלה השדרתית?`
* Answers:
  1. **מקום מושבו של מוח השדרה, העובר דרך ה-Vertebral Foramen** (`correct_answer` = 1)
  2. מבנה של החוליות הצוואריות בלבד
  3. מקום לעצבוב גפיים תחתונות בלבד
  4. חלק מהחוליות המותניות בלבד
* Grounding verdict: **`passed = True`** (`grounded=True`, `correct_answer_supported=True`, `other_answers_not_equally_correct=True`), `confidence = 0.95`.
* Reason (verbatim): "The candidate question about the structure of the spinal canal is supported by the evidence, which states that the spinal canal is the location of the spinal cord passing through the vertebral foramen. The intended correct answer (Answer 1) is also supported by this evidence. Other answers do not have equivalent support, as they either misrepresent the structure or limit it incorrectly to specific vertebrae or functions."
* Supporting evidence ids returned by the validator (all verified against the actual independently-retrieved evidence, per WP-010's provenance check): `STUDENT_SUMMARY:student_summary_2.pdf:0003:0001`, `STUDENT_SUMMARY:student_summary_1.pdf:0003:0001`.
* **Attempt history (recorded honestly, per the WP's explicit instruction not to hide observations)**: this succeeded on the third live generation attempt. The first two attempts (before the prompt fix) failed *during generation* with `InvalidGeneratedOutputError` - not a grounding failure - because the model reported an `evidence_chunk_ids` value with the `SourceType:` prefix dropped, which WP-009's provenance check correctly rejected. See "Decisions Made (WP-010)" for the fix and full reasoning. No attempt was made to re-roll for a "better" question once a structurally valid one was produced; the smoke test stopped at the first successful generate-then-validate pair.
* This is one observation, not a systematic grounding-quality measurement - a `passed=True` result here says nothing about the validator's or generator's behavior across many questions/categories. No prompt/retrieval tuning was performed in response to this specific outcome (there was nothing to react to - it passed).

## Live Candidate-Quality Validation Smoke Test (WP-011)

**Executed successfully**, once each, against a freshly generated candidate (a new generate call, not a reused prior candidate - not readily reproducible from an in-process object across separate WP runs). One real generate-then-validate-four-ways sequence: `QuestionGenerator.from_default_configuration().generate_candidate_question(category=<first real canonical category>, generation_mode=GenerationMode.INDEPENDENT)`, immediately followed by `GroundingValidator`, `MCQValidator`, `CategoryValidator`, and `QualityValidator`, each `.from_default_configuration()`, each invoked exactly once.

* Category used: `התעלה השדרתית ותכולתה` (same first canonical category as the WP-009/WP-010 live tests).
* Generated question: `מהו התפקיד של החוליות בעמוד השדרה?`
* Answers:
  1. לספק מבנה ותמיכה לגוף
  2. למנוע תנועות בלתי רצויות של הגוף
  3. **לשמש כמקום מושב למוח השדרה** (`correct_answer` = 3)
  4. למנוע כאבים בעמוד השדרה
* Results (all four independent, all executed successfully as normal results - no exception raised by any validator):

  | Validator | Verdict | Reason (verbatim) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Grounding | **FAIL** | "The question about the role of vertebrae in the spine is supported by the evidence, which describes the structure and function of vertebrae, including their role in forming the spinal canal. However, the intended correct answer (3) states that vertebrae serve as a seat for the spinal cord, which is not explicitly supported by the evidence. Other answers, such as (1) providing structure and support to the body, are supported by the evidence, making them equally valid. Therefore, the intended correct answer is not the only supported answer." |
  | MCQ | **FAIL** | "More than one answer choice could reasonably be defended as correct. Answers 1 and 3 both provide plausible roles of the vertebrae, making it unclear which is the single best answer." |
  | Category | **FAIL** | "The question focuses on the role of vertebrae in the spine, which does not directly pertain to the spinal canal and its contents." |
  | Quality | **FAIL** | "The question is clear and unambiguous, but the intended correct answer (Answer 3) is factually incorrect... Additionally, Answer 4... could be interpreted as a role of the vertebrae... This creates ambiguity regarding the correct answer." |

* This is a genuinely weak candidate on all four independent axes at once, not a validator malfunction: MCQ correctly flags the same answer-1-vs-3 ambiguity that Grounding independently flags via unsupported-exclusivity, and Category correctly flags that the question is really about general vertebral function rather than the requested "spinal canal and its contents" category. This is exactly the kind of honest, informative multi-axis failure WP-011 explicitly asks to be surfaced rather than hidden - **per WP-011 section 15, this is a successful smoke test of the implementation, not a failed WP.** No prompt was tuned in response, and no repeated generation was attempted to find a "better" candidate.
* Only one live generate-then-validate sequence was made, per this project's established cost/frequency constraint for live smoke tests.

## Live Textbook Validation Smoke Test (WP-012)

**Executed successfully**, once, against a freshly generated candidate (a new generate call, not the WP-011 candidate - not readily reproducible from an in-process object across separate WP runs, per WP-012 section 15's own "if readily reproducible" qualifier). One real generate-then-textbook-validate sequence: `QuestionGenerator.from_default_configuration().generate_candidate_question(category=<first real canonical category>, generation_mode=GenerationMode.INDEPENDENT)`, immediately followed by `TextbookValidator.from_default_configuration().validate(candidate)`, invoked exactly once. Per WP-012 section 15's own instruction not to make extra API calls merely to reconstruct them, the other four (WP-010/WP-011) validators were **not** re-run against this candidate - only the textbook result is reported here.

* Category used: `התעלה השדרתית ותכולתה` (same first canonical category as the WP-009/WP-010/WP-011 live tests).
* Generated question: `מהו המבנה הממוקם בתעלה השדרתית ומה תפקידו?`
* Answers:
  1. **המוח השדרה - מעביר מידע סנסורי ומוטורי** (`correct_answer` = 1)
  2. החוליה - תומכת במבנה השדרה
  3. השרירים - מאפשרים תנועה
  4. העורקים - מספקים דם למוח השדרה
* Course-book evidence ids independently retrieved and supplied to the LLM call (3): `COURSE_BOOK:course_book.pdf:0128:0002`, `COURSE_BOOK:course_book.pdf:0132:0002`, `COURSE_BOOK:course_book.pdf:0119:0002`.
* Textbook verdict: **`NOT_FOUND`** (`source_page=None`, `reference_text=None` - both correctly absent, matching a `NOT_FOUND` status's own semantics of "no confident page/quote to cite").
* Reason (verbatim): "The course-book evidence does not provide relevant information regarding the structure located in the spinal canal or its function."
* This is a legitimate, informative result per WP-012 sections 8/15 explicitly, not an implementation defect: three course-book chunks were retrieved and genuinely considered (the LLM call *was* made - retrieval was not empty), but the model judged them insufficient to confidently support or contradict the specific candidate. No prompt was tuned in response, and no repeated generation/retrieval was attempted to find a "better" result.
* Only one live generate-then-validate call was made, per this project's established cost/frequency constraint for live smoke tests.

## Live Production Cycle Smoke Test (WP-013)

**Executed successfully**, once, using `QuestionProducer.from_default_configuration().produce_question(category=<first real canonical category>, generation_mode=GenerationMode.INDEPENDENT)` with the configured `max_attempts=3` (`config/app.yaml`'s existing `generation.max_generation_attempts`). Ran after the live-test-driven textbook prompt fix described above (the *first* run of this smoke test surfaced that defect via `InvalidTextbookOutputError`, before any candidate reached an acceptance decision; this is the subsequent, complete run).

* Category used: `התעלה השדרתית ותכולתה` (same first canonical category as every prior live test).
* Outcome: **exhausted** - all 3 attempts completed operationally successfully (no exceptions), all 3 rejected by the deterministic acceptance policy. `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError` was raised carrying all 3 completed `QuestionAttempt` records, exactly as designed.

  | Attempt | Grounding | MCQ | Category | Quality | Textbook | Accepted |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | PASS | PASS | PASS | **FAIL** | NOT_FOUND | NO |
  | 2 | PASS | **FAIL** | PASS | **FAIL** | NOT_FOUND | NO |
  | 3 | PASS | **FAIL** | PASS | **FAIL** | NOT_FOUND | NO |

* Representative reasons: Attempt 1 Quality FAIL - "The question lacks clarity and specificity regarding the role of the התעלה השדרתית. The answer choices are ambiguous and do not clearly differentiate the functions of the spinal canal..."; Attempt 2/3 MCQ FAIL - "More than one answer choice could reasonably be defended as correct..."; all three Textbook results were `NOT_FOUND` because independent course-book retrieval returned zero chunks for each of the three generated question phrasings (the empty-retrieval short-circuit path from "Decisions Made (WP-012)" - no LLM call was made for textbook validation on any of the three attempts).
* This is a legitimate, informative "exhausted" result per WP-013 section 20 explicitly ("An exhausted live test is useful baseline data and does not mean WP-013 failed") - not an implementation defect. Grounding passed in all three attempts (generation is reliably producing factually-grounded questions), but MCQ/Quality repeatedly failed on genuine construction weaknesses (ambiguous best-answer, unclear question phrasing) - consistent with, and reinforcing, the same generation-quality weaknesses WP-011's own live smoke test already observed independently. No prompt was tuned in response to this outcome, and the smoke test was not rerun to try to force an accepted result.
* Only one production-cycle invocation was made, per WP-013 section 20's explicit "keep the smoke test to one production cycle" instruction - it nonetheless made 15 real LLM calls under the hood (3 attempts × [1 generation + 4 always-called validators], with the 5th validator, textbook, making zero calls on all three attempts since its own independent retrieval found nothing each time).

## Live Exam Orchestration Smoke Test (WP-014)

**Executed successfully**, once (after the two prompt fixes described above), using `ExamOrchestrator.from_default_configuration().generate_exam(ExamRequest(categories={<category 16>: 1, <category 17>: 1}))` - 2 questions total, per WP-014 section 20's recommended small smoke-test size.

* **Attempt history, reported honestly per this project's established practice**: several earlier attempts against different real category pairs (the 1st/2nd, 3rd/4th, and 10th/11th canonical categories) hit real operational failures before this successful run - see "Decisions Made (WP-014)" above for the full accounting (one genuinely fixed defect, two investigated-but-not-fixed reliability issues in already-approved components). These are not WP-014 orchestration defects: in every case, `ExamOrchestrator`'s own logic behaved exactly as designed (no `try`/`except` swallowed the failure; it propagated immediately, exactly as WP-014 section 18 requires) - the failures originated inside already-approved WP-009/WP-012 components several layers below the orchestrator. The final run used the 16th/17th canonical categories (`אמבריולוגיה`, `טופוגרפיה של ההמיספרות`) and completed cleanly with no retries needed.
* Requested: 2 questions total (`{"אמבריולוגיה": 1, "טופוגרפיה של ההמיספרות": 1}`). Completed: **2/2**.
* Plan: position 1 = `אמבריולוגיה` / `STYLE_SIMILAR`; position 2 = `טופוגרפיה של ההמיספרות` / `STYLE_SIMILAR` (each category's own first question, so both correctly started at `STYLE_SIMILAR` per the per-category-restart policy).
* Per-question production: **both accepted on the first attempt** (1 attempt each, 0 duplicate replacements each) - Grounding/MCQ/Category/Quality all PASS, Textbook `NOT_FOUND` for both (independent course-book retrieval found nothing for either generated question - the empty-retrieval short-circuit path, no LLM call made for textbook validation on either).
* No duplicate replacements were needed or observed.
* Final exam (2 questions, in plan order, numbered 1-2):

  1. **(אמבריולוגיה)** מהו השם של התהליך שבו מתפתח דיסק תלת שכבתי מהדיסק הדו שכבתי במהלך האמבריאולוגיה?
     1. **גסטרולציה** (correct) 2. היפובלסטציה 3. אפיבלסטציה 4. דיפרנציאציה
  2. **(טופוגרפיה של ההמיספרות)** מהו הסולקוס המרכזי המחלק את האונה הפרונטלית מהאונה הפריאטלית במוח?
     1. **סולקוס צנטרלי** (correct) 2. סולקוס פריאטו-אוקסיפיטלי 3. סולקוס סילביאן 4. סולקוס אינטרפריאטלי

* This is a clean, complete, first-attempt success for both planned questions - a useful positive data point (unlike WP-013's own exhausted-baseline observation), though still just one exam/one category pair, not a systematic acceptance-rate measurement. No prompt was tuned to force this specific outcome; the two prompt fixes made beforehand addressed genuinely reproducible defects unrelated to *this* run's content, and category selection changed across attempts only to get a informative complete run for the report (not to cherry-pick an easy pass - several of the earlier, failed attempts used different categories for the same reason: to distinguish "is this categorical/content-specific" from "is this systemic").

## Real-Data Smoke Test (WP-015)

**Executed successfully**, once, using a real `ExamOrchestrator.from_default_configuration().generate_exam()` call (2 questions, categories 16/17 - the same pair that produced WP-014's clean live result) piped directly into `build_exam_output_bundle()`. WP-015 itself makes zero LLM calls; the only live API usage in this smoke test is the WP-014 exam-generation step that produces the `ExamGenerationResult` WP-015 then transforms. The live exam generation succeeded cleanly on the first attempt (no operational failures this run, unlike several of WP-014's own earlier attempts) - a realistic-fixture fallback (explicitly permitted by WP-015 section 20) was not needed.

* Clean exam (`bundle.exam`, `serialize_exam_json()`): 2 questions, numbered 1-2, categories `אמבריולוגיה`/`טופוגרפיה של ההמיספרות`, Hebrew fully readable (no `\uXXXX` escaping), no validation/generation-mode/evidence field present anywhere in the JSON (structurally impossible - `ExamQuestion` has no such fields).
* Audit (`bundle.audit`, `serialize_audit_json()`): 2 corresponding `QuestionAudit` records (`exam_id="exam-wp015-smoke"`, `provider="openai"`, `model="gpt-4o-mini"`), each with a complete `attempts` list (1 attempt each, both accepted on the first try, matching the WP-014 result), all five validation results present per attempt, `historical_reference_id: null` and `evidence: []` (the honestly-represented gaps - see "Decisions Made (WP-015)"), grounding-evidence identity intact and visible (`grounding.evidence_chunk_ids` - three real `STUDENT_SUMMARY:...` chunk ids for question 1's embryology grounding), and `textbook_check.status: "NOT_FOUND"` for both (empty course-book retrieval, matching WP-012's/WP-014's own short-circuit design).
* Cross-consistency check passed silently (no `AuditConsistencyError`) - confirming, on real (not synthetic) data, that `build_exam_audit()`'s invariant checks hold for actual `ExamOrchestrator` output, not just the hand-constructed fixtures used in unit tests.
* `AuditConsistencyError`'s failure-path behavior (mismatched question count/text/category/correct-answer, and rejected-attempt-as-accepted) was verified only via the unit-test fixtures in `tests/unit/test_output.py`, not against a real tampered result - deliberately constructing a real inconsistent `ExamGenerationResult` live would require no additional LLM calls to test meaningfully (the check operates on already-in-memory data), so no live coverage was needed here.
* No file-writing (`write_exam_json`/`write_audit_json`) was exercised against real output paths during this specific smoke run (only in the offline test suite, via `tmp_path`) - deferred to a natural point once WP-016's CLI has a real caller-supplied output location; the serialization functions those file-writers call (`serialize_exam_json`/`serialize_audit_json`) were exercised directly against this real data.

## CLI Smoke Test (WP-016)

**Executed successfully**, once, running the real installed-equivalent command (`python -m exam_generator.cli generate --request ... --exam-output ... --audit-output ...`) end-to-end against the real OpenAI API - the first genuinely full pipeline run through the actual CLI entry point (not Python API calls), with 1 requested question (category `אמבריולוגיה`, per WP-016 section 21's "prefer 1 question to limit cost").

* Exit code: **0**. Console output showed the expected stage sequence (`Loading request...` / `Initializing exam generator...` / `Generating exam...` / `Writing exam output...` / `Writing audit output...` / `Done.`) followed by the success summary (`Exam generated successfully: 1 question(s)`, both output paths).
* Both `exam.json` and `audit.json` were created, both valid UTF-8 JSON, Hebrew fully readable in both (no `\uXXXX` escaping) - confirmed by `json.load()` round-tripping successfully and a direct substring check.
* Clean exam contained exactly the public `ExamQuestion` fields (`number`, `question`, `answer1..4`, `correct_answer`, `category`) - no audit internals present.
* Audit corresponded to the generated question: same category/question/answers, `exam_id` derived from the run's timestamp, `provider="openai"`/`model="gpt-4o-mini"`, and - a genuinely useful real-data finding - **`generation_attempts: 2`** (the first attempt was rejected, `attempts[0].accepted == false`; the second was accepted, `attempts[1].accepted == true`), directly confirming that WP-013's bounded quality-regeneration loop, WP-016's "don't write output until the whole pipeline succeeds" guarantee, and WP-015's full-attempt-history preservation all worked correctly together on one real run - the CLI reported only the final accepted result cleanly, while the audit still preserved the rejected first attempt's complete validation detail.
* No secrets (`OPENAI_API_KEY` value, "sk-" prefix) appeared anywhere in either output file - confirmed by direct grep.
* One real invocation was made, per the WP's explicit "one real invocation is enough" instruction; no repeated reruns were performed to chase a different outcome.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-026)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-026) → **1024 passed**, up from 1010 (WP-025's own baseline), zero network access.
* Focused live 4-target control run: reused the exact four `QuestionTarget`s that failed in WP-025's own acceptance run (per the WP-025A diagnostic) - `4/4 accepted` (WP-025A baseline: 3/4, with the fourth exhausted twice, 6/6 attempts total across both prior runs). The previously-reproducibly-failing PNS-divisions case (adjacent-hierarchy-level ambiguity between "autonomic/somatic" and "sympathetic/parasympathetic") succeeded on attempt 2 this time, using explicit "basic division" hierarchy-level wording. A supplementary informal second production call on that same target (run afterward, for completion-report detail only, not part of the sanctioned control run) exhausted again with the identical ambiguity recurring - recorded honestly: the fix meaningfully reduces but does not fully eliminate this specific case's stochasticity.
* Live full 40-question acceptance run (WP-026): 20 categories × 2 questions against the real OpenAI API - `status=PARTIAL`, **35/40 accepted, 5 failed** (all `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError`). Runtime ~22.5 minutes. **None of the 5 failures match the enumeration/list-recall pattern WP-026 targeted** - the causes were category-boundary ambiguity (2 cases: a target's natural content sat at the edge between two canonical categories), a genuine textbook-terminology conflict (1 case: dura mater layer naming), and ordinary MCQ ambiguity unrelated to enumeration (2 cases, including one where generation narrowed to an embryological-origin property that itself turned out ambiguous). Accepted-on-attempt distribution: **27/4/4** (attempt 1/2/3), avg 1.34 attempts/accepted question - both improved over WP-025's 23/7/6 (avg 1.53). Diversity review of all 15 category pairs with both questions accepted: **15/15 DISTINCT, 0 borderline, 0 duplicate**. Human review of accepted questions from enumeration/classification-shaped targets found the intended "one member + one distinguishing property" framing used consistently and correctly in nearly all cases (e.g. the white-matter-fibers target reproduced the prompt's own worked example almost verbatim) - **with one notable exception**: question #23 (מערכת העצבים ההיקפית, "which system is part of PNS") used a "which one IS a member" framing against a 2-item target (autonomic + somatic) and offered the sibling member (autonomic) as an incorrect distractor, even though the assigned target's own evidence names it as an equally genuine PNS component - all three relevant validators (grounding, MCQ, quality) approved it regardless. This slipped through despite WP-026's distractor-construction guidance, and is documented as a known residual gap rather than fixed post-hoc (no tuning after the run). See `implementation/WP-026_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full analysis.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-027)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-027) → **1050 passed**, up from 1024 (WP-026's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical (grounding models are never schema-exported).
* Re-evaluation of the 5 persisted WP-026 suspicious candidates (#8, #14, #23, #32, #30 - exact candidate text/answers, not regenerated) through the strengthened `GroundingValidator`: **#8 and #23 (the two `CONFIRMED_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER` cases) and #32 (one of the two `POSSIBLE` cases) now correctly fail** (`other_answers_not_equally_correct=False`); **#14 (the more genuinely ambiguous `POSSIBLE` case) and #30 (a different defect shape - an unsupported-designated-answer concern, not distractor-correctness) still pass**, confirming the strengthened validator targets the actual defect without over-rejecting a case outside its scope.
* Focused live test (3 targets, full generation+validation pipeline, reusing the exact WP-026 targets for the #8/#23/#32 shapes): **all 3 accepted** (attempts 3/2/1 respectively) with genuinely single-answer framings this time - #23 flipped to "which of the following constitutes the somatic part of PNS" (autonomic/sympathetic/parasympathetic all cleanly wrong for that specific framing); #32 combined both real microglia functions (phagocytosis + synapse clearance) into one correct answer rather than presenting them as competing options.
* Live full 40-question acceptance run (WP-027): 20 categories × 2 questions against the real OpenAI API - `status=PARTIAL`, **34/40 accepted, 6 failed** (all `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError`). Runtime ~22.9 minutes. Accepted-on-attempt distribution: **27/5/2** (attempt 1/2/3), avg 1.26 attempts/accepted question. Validator rejection counts (all rejected attempts): grounding 12, quality 10, mcq 8, textbook 6, category 3. **Grounding rejection breakdown: all 12 were "another answer also supported" (0 designated-unsupported, 0 premise-unsupported)** - the strengthened per-option check is actively engaged at scale, not merely theoretically capable. **False-acceptance human review of all 34 accepted questions: 33 CLEAR_SINGLE_ANSWER, 1 POSSIBLE_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER (a genuine real-world domain subtlety - "Diffusion Imaging" is technically an MRI modality, though the supplied evidence treated them as distinct named techniques), 0 CONFIRMED_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER, 0 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE_TO_JUDGE - meeting WP-027's primary quality goal (confirmed=0).** Several accepted questions (e.g. #24, #26, #27, #28) tested one member of an enumeration-shaped target against genuine sibling distractors, but succeeded because the question's added specific property (a function, a location, a system membership) cleanly excluded the siblings - direct evidence the WP-026+WP-027 combination works together, not just each fix in isolation. Diversity review of all 14 category pairs with both questions accepted: **14/14 DISTINCT, 0 borderline, 0 duplicate**. Accepted count is one lower than WP-026's 34-vs-35 - expected and reported honestly per the WP's own anticipated reliability trade-off: the strengthened validator now correctly rejects cases the old one would likely have waved through. See `implementation/WP-027_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full analysis.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-028)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-028) → **1070 passed**, up from 1050 (WP-027's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: byte-identical (blueprint models are never schema-exported).
* Focused live evaluation (6 candidates across `מערכת העצבים ההיקפית`, `אספקת דם`, `תאי מערכת העצבים` - the categories with historically weak distractors): **5/6 accepted**, avg 1.67 attempts. The one exhaustion (PNS divisions - the same target that has proven hard across WP-025A/026/027 too) failed for a **different** reason than the multi-correct-answer pattern: two attempts contained outright factual errors in distractor content (e.g. claiming the autonomic system controls voluntary skeletal muscle, which is simply false), caught correctly by quality/category/MCQ; the third was a category-scope rejection. No false-acceptance-shaped failure occurred in this focused sample.
* **First full acceptance-run attempt crashed before completing** (27/40 questions produced, then `LLMStructuredOutputError` at question 34: a `GroundingValidationResponse` was truncated mid-JSON, exhausting WP-020's existing bounded physical retry - correctly classified system-level by design, matching pre-existing test expectations, and aborting the whole run). This is a known, pre-existing risk in the WP-020 retry mechanism, unrelated to WP-028's own generation-only changes (the truncation was in the *grounding* response, not the *generation* response the blueprint modified) - not a WP-028 regression, and not something tuned away; a second full run was performed since the first did not produce a valid completed measurement to report at all.
* Live full 40-question acceptance run (WP-028, second attempt): 20 categories × 2 questions - `status=PARTIAL`, **31/40 accepted, 9 failed** (all `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError`). Runtime ~27.8 minutes. Accepted-on-attempt distribution: **23/5/3**, avg **1.35** attempts/accepted question - both **worse** than WP-027's 27/5/2 (avg 1.26). Validator rejection counts: mcq 18, quality 15, grounding 16, category 6, textbook 2 - **mcq/quality/grounding/category all rose relative to WP-027** (8→18, 10→15, 12→16, 3→6); only textbook fell (6→2). **Grounding rejection breakdown: 14 "another answer also supported," 2 "designated answer unsupported"** - the WP's own explicitly-stated primary success metric ("grounding rejections caused by another supported answer should decrease") **did not improve; it moved in the wrong direction** (14 vs. WP-027's 12), reported honestly rather than reframed. **False-acceptance human review of all 31 accepted questions: 29 CLEAR_SINGLE_ANSWER, 2 POSSIBLE_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER (both genuine real-world anatomical-overlap subtleties - PCA/SCA both plausibly "supply posterior CNS regions" per general anatomy, and venous sinuses genuinely drain via both the jugular vein and emissary veins), 0 CONFIRMED_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER** - correctness held at WP-027's own bar even though efficiency did not improve. Diversity review of the 12 category pairs with both questions accepted (fewer than WP-027's 14, since more categories had only one or zero accepted this run): **12/12 DISTINCT**. See `implementation/WP-028_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full analysis, including the leading hypothesis for the regression (the blueprint's `evidence_checked` and other fields are the model's own self-reported, never independently-verified claims - unlike WP-027's per-option grounding assessment, which is checked by a separate validator call).

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-030)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-030) → **1111 passed**, up from 1070 (WP-028's own baseline; WP-029 was design-only and added no tests), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: byte-identical (relationship models are never schema-exported).
* Focused live evaluation (the same 6-candidate, 3-category methodology as WP-028's own focused eval - `מערכת העצבים ההיקפית`, `אספקת דם`, `תאי מערכת העצבים`): **6/6 accepted, avg 1.00 attempts** - a clean sweep, including a fresh PNS-divisions target (the historically hardest recurring case), better than WP-028's 5/6 (avg 1.67).
* Live full 40-question acceptance run (WP-030): 20 categories × 2 questions - `status=PARTIAL`, **32/40 accepted, 8 failed** (all `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError`). Runtime ~29.7 minutes. Accepted-on-attempt distribution: **25/3/4**, avg **1.34** attempts/accepted question. Validator rejection counts: mcq 9, quality 3, grounding 24, category 8, textbook 0. **Grounding rejection breakdown: all 24 were "another answer also supported"** - the WP's own primary success metric (this count should decrease relative to WP-027's 12) **did not improve; it nearly doubled**, and is worse than even WP-028's 14. **However, MCQ rejections returned to WP-027-like levels (9, vs. WP-027's 8 and WP-028's 18) and quality rejections fell further (3, vs. WP-027's 10 and WP-028's 15)** - a genuine secondary improvement, consistent with the relationship framing helping structural/wording clarity without WP-028's unconstrained-archetype-selection cost. Only 17 of 40 planned targets (42.5%) actually matched a known relationship keyword this run; among the 13 planned positions with at least one "another also supported" rejection, the relationship-classified ones (`CONTAINS`/`DEVELOPS_INTO`) did not fail at a meaningfully higher rate than `UNSPECIFIED` ones - the grounding regression is not clearly attributable to the relationship layer itself, more likely ordinary run-to-run target-difficulty variance (this is a single-run observation, not a controlled comparison). **False-acceptance human review of all 32 accepted questions: 30 CLEAR_SINGLE_ANSWER, 2 POSSIBLE_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER, 0 CONFIRMED_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER** - one of the two `POSSIBLE` cases is a notable, direct recurrence of the original WP-025A/026 enumeration-ambiguity shape (a target's own `factual_focus` explicitly grouped three sibling neural-tube-flexure events under one shared broad relationship - "occurring during week 5" - while the accepted question designated only one of the three correct). Diversity review of the 13 category pairs with both questions accepted: **13/13 DISTINCT**. See `implementation/WP-030_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full analysis and open questions about whether WP-029's Phase 2/3 should proceed as originally scoped.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-031)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-031) → **1131 passed**, up from 1111 (WP-030's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: byte-identical (competitor models are never schema-exported).
* Focused live evaluation (same 6-candidate, 3-category methodology as WP-030's): **5/6 accepted, avg 2.00 attempts** - the recurring PNS-divisions target exhausted again; its `factual_focus` uses a verb form ("מחולק ל") not covered by the current relationship-keyword table, so it classified `UNSPECIFIED` and received no competitor support either - an honest, expected limitation, not a WP-031 regression (WP-030's own report already flagged this exact coverage gap).

Accepted-on-attempt distribution: **28/3/1** - the best first-attempt rate of any run so far - avg **1.16** attempts/accepted question, also the best of any run (WP-027: 1.26, WP-028: 1.35, WP-030: 1.34). Validator rejection counts: mcq 14, quality 10, category 9, grounding 16, textbook 0. **Grounding rejection breakdown: all 16 were "another answer also supported" - down from WP-030's 24 (a real, measured improvement), though still above WP-027's original 12.** An offline investigation cross-referencing relationship classification against the 8 positions with a grounding failure found 3 were relationship-classified and 5 were `UNSPECIFIED` - proportionally similar to each group's overall representation (15 classified / 40, 25 unclassified / 40), so the improvement is not cleanly attributable to competitor discovery alone from this single run's data, though the overall direction (fewer failing positions: 8 vs. WP-030's 13; fewer total grounding failures: 16 vs. 24) is real. **False-acceptance human review of all 32 accepted questions: 31 CLEAR_SINGLE_ANSWER, 1 POSSIBLE_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER, 0 CONFIRMED_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER** - the cleanest correctness result of any run so far, tying WP-027. Diversity review of the 12 category pairs with both questions accepted: **12/12 DISTINCT**. See `implementation/WP-031_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full analysis and the explicit architectural evaluation of whether competitor discovery improved generation quality.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-032)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-032) → **1153 passed**, up from 1131 (WP-031's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: byte-identical (`CategoryGenerationRequest`/`Response` are new `category_generation/` models, never schema-exported - `ExamRequest`/`ExamOutput`/`ExamAudit` were not touched by this WP).
* **Architectural smoke test (section 10)**: one category (`אספקת דם`), four sequential `CategoryGenerationService.generate_next()` calls with 0, then 1, then 2, then 3 `existing_questions` - **4/4 accepted**, confirming the new API behaves correctly, generation still succeeds, and the output contract remains valid (not a diversity evaluation, per section 10's own scope). Rounds 2 and 3 converged on near-identical questions ("which artery supplies the superior [surface] of the cerebellum" - superior cerebellar artery, twice), an early, honest signal of the diversity-granularity effect the full acceptance run below confirmed at scale.
* **Live full 40-question acceptance run (WP-032), exclusively through the new architecture** (`CategoryGenerationRequest` → `CategoryGenerationService.generate_next()` → append accepted question → repeat, per section 11 - never through `ExamOrchestrator`/the CLI): 20 categories × 2 questions - **36/40 accepted, 4 failed** (all `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError`; one category, `מערכת העצבים ההיקפית`, lost both positions). This alone confirms the new architecture can completely replace the old orchestration end-to-end (WP-032's own stated acceptance-run purpose, section 11) - every planned call produced a structurally valid `CategoryGenerationResponse`, and every accepted question round-tripped through the same five validators/acceptance policy unchanged.
* **Accepted count itself improved** (36/40 vs. WP-031's 32/40), **but every efficiency and diversity metric regressed**, and the two are very likely connected. Accepted-on-attempt distribution: **23/5/8**, avg **1.58** attempts/accepted question - worse than every prior run (WP-027: 1.26, WP-028: 1.35, WP-030: 1.34, WP-031: 1.16). First-attempt rate 23/36 (63.9%) - also worse than WP-031's 87.5%. Validator rejection counts: mcq 12, quality 11, category 4, grounding 22, textbook 1. **Grounding rejection breakdown: 20 "another answer also supported," 2 "designated answer unsupported"** - the "another answer also supported" count rose to 20 (vs. WP-031's 16), moving in the wrong direction, though still better than WP-030's 24.
* **Diversity regressed sharply and is the run's most important finding.** Of the 17 category pairs with both questions accepted, only **7/17 (41%) were DISTINCT**, 3/17 BORDERLINE, and **7/17 (41%) DUPLICATE or near-duplicate** - a stark reversal from WP-031's 12/12 DISTINCT, WP-030's 13/13, and WP-027's 14/14. Concrete duplicate pairs included: `לוקליזציה פונקציונלית` (BA4 ↔ precentral gyrus, tested in both directions), `עצבים קרניאליים` (olfactory nerve/telencephalon-origin, reworded), `אספקת דם` (superior cerebellar artery, reworded - the same convergence the smoke test already flagged), `מסילות עצביות` (spinothalamic tract, reworded), `המוח הקטן` (cerebellar motor coordination, reworded), `אמבריולוגיה` (gastrulation, reworded), and `תאי מערכת העצבים` (microglia phagocytosis, overlapping). **Leading, honestly-labeled hypothesis (not proven from a single run)**: WP-032 narrows target planning from "once per category, `count=N`, mutually co-aware" to "once per `generate_next()` call, `count=1`, blind to sibling targets already used for the same category" - an inherent, disclosed structural consequence of the new per-question API (see `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s new WP-032 section), not a deliberate diversity change (explicitly out of scope, section 13). The higher accepted count is plausibly the *same* cause manifesting differently: without co-planned distinct targets, planning may gravitate toward the most clearly evidence-supported, "safest" facts repeatedly (e.g. superior cerebellar artery, spinothalamic tract) - easier to ground and validate, but at the cost of testing the same content twice.
* **False-acceptance human review of all 36 accepted questions: 34 CLEAR_SINGLE_ANSWER, 2 POSSIBLE_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER, 0 CONFIRMED_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER** - correctness held at the project's established bar even amid the diversity regression (a genuinely separate axis: false-acceptance measures whether an *individual* accepted question has a second correct answer, not whether two *different* accepted questions overlap in content).
* **This regression is a real, measured, negative side effect of WP-032's architecture change, not tuned away or hidden** - per WP-032 section 13 ("do NOT improve diversity... those belong to later work packages") fixing it is explicitly out of scope for this WP; reporting it honestly is not. See `implementation/WP-032_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-category diversity/false-acceptance tables and the open question of how a future WP should restore category-level target co-awareness within the new per-question API shape.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-033)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-033) → **1179 passed**, up from 1153 (WP-032's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: `schemas/exam_output.schema.json` **changed** (`ExamQuestion.number` became optional - no longer in `required`, now `anyOf[int>0, null]`, default `null`) - expected and disclosed, not accidental; `schemas/exam_request.schema.json`/`exam_audit.schema.json` byte-identical (unaffected, as expected).
* **Architectural smoke test (section 9, scenarios A-D)**: one category (`אספקת דם`), four sequential `CategoryQuestionSetService.generate_next()` calls with 0, then 1, then 2, then 3 `existing_questions` (`ExamQuestion`-shaped) - **4/4 accepted**, confirming the new request/response contract behaves correctly, generation still succeeds, and every generated question uses exactly the production schema (`ExamQuestion`, `number=None` in every case, confirming section 4's "generation is responsible only for the question content"). All four scenarios' generated questions tested the same underlying fact ("which artery supplies the superior surface of the cerebellum," reworded four times) - the same target-planning-granularity convergence WP-032 already found and disclosed, unsurprising since WP-033 does not touch target planning at all (section 11 explicitly excludes "analyze existing questions"/"compute coverage"/"improve diversity") - not a new finding, not evaluated further per section 9's own "do not evaluate diversity" scope note.
* **No full 40-question acceptance run was performed for WP-033** - unlike WP-032, WP-033's own spec has no "Acceptance Run" section requiring one (only "Section 10: Acceptance Criteria," a bullet list of success conditions, and "Section 9: Evaluation," the four-scenario architectural smoke test above) - running one anyway would have been unrequested scope/cost, and section 9 explicitly frames the four scenarios as "only an architectural smoke test." The terminal "Acceptance run" summary line reports the four-scenario evaluation's result.
* **Backward compatibility was directly tested, not assumed**: `tests/unit/test_category_question_set.py` includes explicit tests constructing and exercising `CategoryGenerationService`/`CategoryGenerationRequest` (WP-032's own contract) after the WP-033 refactor, confirming both the accepted-candidate and question-local-failure paths still behave identically - `CategoryGenerationService` was not modified in observable behavior at all, only its internal implementation (now delegating to the shared `_run_generation_cycle()` helper it also shares with `CategoryQuestionSetService`).

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-034)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-034) → **1206 passed**, up from 1179 (WP-033's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical (`CategoryCoverage` is a deliberately internal-only model, never schema-exported).
* **Evaluation (section 10)**: four sequential questions in the exact category WP-033's own evaluation used (`אספקת דם`), via `CategoryQuestionSetService`, coverage now threaded into every planning call. **4/4 accepted, but zero diversity improvement**: all four questions converged on the identical underlying fact ("Superior Cerebellar Artery supplies the cerebellum") - the coverage section correctly and verifiably listed this exact concept and the `SUPPLIES` relationship type as already-tested from the second call onward (confirmed by inspecting `QuestionTargetPlanner.plan_history`), yet the planner re-selected an overlapping target every time; round 3 needed 2 duplicate-replacement attempts before landing on a differently-worded but conceptually identical question. This is the same category, same convergence pattern, same outcome as WP-033's own four-scenario evaluation - coverage-awareness had no measurable effect here.
* **Live full 40-question acceptance run (WP-034), via `CategoryQuestionSetService` only, coverage-aware planning active throughout**: 20 categories × 2 questions - `status`-equivalent **33/40 accepted, 7 failed** (all `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError`; 2 categories - `גזע המוח`, `מערכת העצבים ההיקפית` - lost both positions). Accepted-on-attempt distribution: **23/5/5**, avg **1.45** attempts/accepted question (better than WP-032's 1.58). First-attempt rate 23/33 (69.7%, better than WP-032's 63.9%). Validator rejection counts: mcq 7, quality 7, category 5, grounding 24, textbook 0. **Grounding rejection breakdown: 23 "another answer also supported," 1 "designated answer unsupported"** - this count rose slightly to 23 (vs. WP-032's 20), moving in the wrong direction. Accepted count itself (33/40) is lower than WP-032's 36/40.
* **Diversity review of the 15 category pairs with both questions accepted: 7/15 (47%) DISTINCT, 2/15 BORDERLINE, 6/15 (40%) DUPLICATE** - only marginally better than WP-032's regressed 7/17 (41%) DISTINCT baseline, a difference well within run-to-run noise at this sample size (15-17 pairs), and the DUPLICATE rate barely moved (41%→40%). One category (`מסילות עצביות`) that duplicated in WP-032/033 (spinothalamic tract tested twice) was DISTINCT this run (spinothalamic vs. medial lemniscus) - but categories with a single dominant, highly-salient fact (`אספקת דם`, `חומר לבן`, `אמבריולוגיה`, `תאי מערכת העצבים`, `מבוא`, `לוקליזציה פונקציונלית`) duplicated regardless of coverage information being present and correctly rendered.
* **Explicit architectural evaluation (WP-034 section 14 - required): did using `existing_questions` improve planning? No, not meaningfully.** Coverage is correctly and deterministically computed (directly unit-tested) and correctly delivered into the planning prompt as a clearly-labeled, honestly-rendered "already-tested" section (directly unit-tested, and directly observed in the live evaluation above) - the wiring itself works exactly as designed. But the *planner's actual target-selection behavior* did not meaningfully change: acceptance-run diversity moved from 41% to 47% DISTINCT (statistically indistinguishable at this sample size) and the controlled same-category evaluation showed zero improvement at all. **What appears to be missing**: soft, information-only prompt guidance ("avoid this if a genuine alternative exists") does not reliably override the model's tendency to select the single most salient, most clearly evidence-supported fact in a category - especially in categories where the evidence has one dominant fact and the alternatives are comparatively weaker or less directly stated. A stronger mechanism (e.g. deterministically excluding or de-weighting already-tested evidence before retrieval reaches the planning prompt, rather than only *informing* the same unconstrained retrieval) might work better, but is explicitly out of WP-034's own scope (section 13 forbids redesigning retrieval, and section 6 forbids turning coverage into a validator/rejection mechanism) - a genuine, honestly-reported limitation of what this WP's own scope allowed it to try, not a design flaw. See `implementation/WP-034_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full data and per-category diversity table.

**WP-035 (investigation only, no production code) concluded deterministic concept ownership is feasible for a subset of categories with clean, list-structured evidence** - see `implementation/WP-035_ARCHITECTURE_INVESTIGATION.md`. No test/live-eval baseline section applies to it.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-036)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-036) → **1235 passed**, up from 1206 (WP-034's own baseline; WP-035 added no tests), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical (`InventoryConcept` is a deliberately internal-only model, never schema-exported).
* **Pilot evaluation/acceptance run (sections 10/11, one combined live test - no reruns)**: four sequential questions per pilot category (`אספקת דם`, `מסילות עצביות`, `גרעיני הבסיס`), via `CategoryQuestionSetService`, concept-inventory-constrained planning active for all three (zero LLM calls made for target planning in any of the 12 rounds). **Accepted: `אספקת דם` 4/4, `מסילות עצביות` 4/4, `גרעיני הבסיס` 3/4** (one `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError`) - comparable to or better than each category's own WP-034 baseline (2/2, 2/2, 1/2 respectively, on half as many planned questions).
* **Target *selection* worked exactly as designed - but the diversity problem was not fixed, for a newly-identified, more specific reason than WP-034's finding.** Every round was correctly assigned a different, coverage-filtered concept (confirmed via `plan_history`) - yet the *actual tested content* repeatedly reverted to the same dominant fact anyway: `אספקת דם` assigned "Basillar artery" in rounds 2, 3, and 4 (each time correctly excluded from the prior round's *nominal* target), but all four accepted questions' correct answer was "Superior Cerebellar Artery," every round - the target's `factual_focus` context window, extracted from a passage still dominated by that neighboring, more salient entity, did not anchor generation to the assigned concept. `גרעיני הבסיס` reassigned the identical concept ("The Basal Gang" - a PDF-line-wrap-truncated fragment of "The Basal Ganglia," itself essentially a restatement of the category's own name) in **all four rounds without exception**, since generation produced broad category-level questions whose answers never textually matched it.
* **Root cause, evidence-grounded, not speculative: coverage exclusion operates on what generation actually produced, not on what planning intended to assign** - a deliberate WP-034 design choice (coverage must come from real accepted-question text, never a target's own unverified claim) that here reveals a new failure mode: when generation's actual answer diverges from the assigned target's nominal identity, the assigned concept is never marked tested, and - since assignment always proceeds by fixed inventory order - the identical concept is deterministically reassigned next round, reproducing the identical failure. **This is more specific and more actionable than WP-034's finding** ("informing the LLM doesn't work"): here, the LLM's nominal target was genuinely *constrained*, not merely informed, and it still didn't fully work - the missing piece is that the supporting evidence text must unambiguously anchor the question to the assigned concept, which requires either better-isolated context-window extraction or prompt changes, both beyond this pilot's explicit scope (section 13 forbids prompt redesign).
* **Explicit architectural evaluation (WP-036 section 14 - required): was extraction reliable?** Partially - clean for well-isolated list items (`Spinothalamic Tract`, `Superior cerebellar artery`), unreliable at the boundaries (PDF-line-wrap truncation producing `"The Basal Gang"`/`"edial Lemniscus Tract"`, both directly observed causing downstream selection problems). **Did concept-constrained planning improve diversity? No** - selection diversified; tested content did not. **Did reliability regress?** No - accepted counts matched or exceeded each pilot category's WP-034 baseline. **Should this expand beyond the pilot? No, not as currently designed** - the context-window-anchoring gap identified here would only compound across more categories; a future WP would need to address it first (see `implementation/WP-036_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`'s explicit recommendation). See that report for the full per-round data.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-037)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-037) → **1259 passed**, up from 1235 (WP-036's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical (no public contract touched).
* **Live pilot (section 23, one live test, no reruns, no manual repair)**: same three pilot categories, four sequential questions each, via `CategoryQuestionSetService`, `refine_concept_inventory()`/`anchor_concept_evidence()` active for all three. **Accepted: `אספקת דם` 2/4, `גרעיני הבסיס` 2/4, `מסילות עצביות` 4/4** - combined 8/12, down from WP-036's 11/12 for the same three categories (see root cause below).
* **Target alignment improved substantially on manual review - the pilot's central hypothesis is supported.** All 8 accepted questions were manually reviewed for whether the actual correct answer semantically matches the assigned concept (not merely text-exact comparison): **7/8 (87.5%) ALIGNED** - `גרעיני הבסיס` round 3 (assigned "Corpos Str[iatum]," answered with a functional description rather than the named structure) was the only clear misalignment. This is a marked improvement over WP-036's own manually-reclassified alignment rate of roughly 5/11 (45%) for the same three categories.
* **However, a naive automated text-exact alignment check would have shown only 2/8 (25%) "aligned" - because generation frequently answered in Hebrew** (e.g. selected concept "Superior cerebellar artery" → accepted answer "עורק סופריור צרבלרי," a Hebrew transliteration) rather than reusing the English concept name verbatim. **This is the run's central, newly-surfaced finding**: WP-034's coverage mechanism performs *exact* text matching between a target's topic and later accepted-answer text; when generation answers in a different language/script than the assigned concept's own text, coverage silently fails to recognize the concept as tested, so the identical concept is deterministically reselected next round - producing near-duplicate Hebrew-phrased questions that the completely unmodified WP-014 duplicate-replacement mechanism then correctly (and honestly) rejected as `DuplicateReplacementExhausted`, accounting for the accepted-count drop. **Target *selection* diversity therefore regressed relative to WP-036** (same concept reselected repeatedly, this time for a different reason than WP-036's - a coverage-recognition gap, not a generation-drift gap).
* **Comparison table (WP-036 vs. WP-037, same three categories):**

  | Metric | WP-036 | WP-037 |
  |---|---:|---:|
  | Target selection diversity | High (correctly rotated every round) | Low (stuck on the same concept per category most rounds - new cause: coverage-recognition gap) |
  | Target alignment rate (manual) | ~45% (5/11) | ~87.5% (7/8) |
  | Accepted count | 11/12 | 8/12 |
  | Avg attempts (accepted only) | ~1.27 | ~1.13 |
  | Inventory-quality issues | "The Basal Gang" (self-restatement, unaddressed); "edial Lemniscus Tract" (truncated, unaddressed) | Both addressed (excluded / correctly reconstructed); "Corpos Str" (a different, trailing-truncation shape) newly observed, still unaddressed |
  | Anchoring failures | N/A (wide window, not yet introduced) | None outright - anchoring always produced non-empty, on-topic text; quality varied |

* **Explicit architectural evaluation (WP-037 section - required): the anchoring fix worked; a previously-invisible interaction with WP-034's exact-text coverage matching did not.** This maps closest to WP-037's own "Outcome B" (anchoring improves alignment, but a different reliability gap remains) rather than a clean "Outcome A" success. **Recommendation for whoever picks up next**: before any further category expansion, address the Hebrew/English answer-language inconsistency's interaction with coverage matching - options include normalizing both sides of the coverage comparison (e.g. comparing against evidence-anchored terms in both languages) or constraining generation's answer language more explicitly for pilot categories (the latter edges toward prompt changes, out of this WP's own scope). See `implementation/WP-037_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data and manual alignment methodology.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-038)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-038) → **1286 passed**, up from 1259 (WP-037's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical (`ConceptIdentity` is a deliberately internal-only model, never schema-exported).
* **Investigation before implementation (section 4/7/8, required)**: the actual retrieved evidence behind all three WP-037 live-pilot concepts was inspected directly for explicit bilingual pairing before any code was written. None was found for any of the three - see `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s new WP-038 section for the per-category detail. This directly shaped the design: only a narrow, structural, evidence-adjacency mechanism was implemented (never a general transliteration/fuzzy matcher), and the live pilot below independently confirms the same finding at scale (12 rounds, not just the initial static check).
* **Live pilot (section 17, one live test, no reruns, no manual repair)**: same three pilot categories, four sequential questions each, via `CategoryQuestionSetService`, `ConceptIdentity`-based coverage exclusion active for all three (WP-037's anchoring algorithm itself untouched). **Accepted: `אספקת דם` 4/4, `גרעיני הבסיס` 2/4, `מסילות עצביות` 4/4** - combined **10/12**, between WP-036's 11/12 and WP-037's 8/12 for the same three categories.
* **Primary success criterion (section 19) was not met for these concepts - Outcome C, honestly reported, not worked around.** `concept_explicitly_supported_language_forms` was empty for every one of the 12 rounds' selected concepts - the evidence-derived cross-script mechanism never found a safe form to recognize, confirming the pre-implementation investigation live. `אספקת דם` and `גרעיני הבסיס` reproduced WP-037's exact regression: the same concept was selected every round, and Hebrew-language answers were never recognized as covering their English-named concept, because there was nothing safe to recognize them by. `אספקת דם`'s own four Hebrew answers used **three different renderings of the same concept** across the run ("עורק סופריאורי צרבלרי" ×2, "עורק צרבלרי עליון" - a translation, not a transliteration - ×1, "עורק סופריור צרבלרי" ×1), independently reinforcing why a transliteration-matching approach was not attempted (section 24).
* **`מסילות עצביות` genuinely rotated through three distinct concepts (Spinothalamic Tract -> Medial Lemniscus Tract -> Anterior Corticospinal Tract) - its best diversity result across WP-036/037/038 - but this is the pre-existing WP-034/036 normalized-exact-match capability correctly preserved, not new WP-038 capability**: both successful rotations occurred in rounds where generation happened to answer in English, exact-matching the assigned concept's own text.
* **A second, previously-undocumented, same-script coverage-recognition failure mode was surfaced live**: round 3 selected "Anterior Corticospinal T" - truncated by the same already-disclosed, still-unaddressed WP-037 "trailing truncation" gap - and generation correctly answered with the complete form, "Anterior Corticospinal Tract." Because the assigned concept's own stored text is itself incomplete, this correct, well-aligned answer was not recognized as covering it, and the identical (truncated) concept was reselected for round 4. This is a distinct root cause from the cross-script problem (a same-script completeness mismatch, not a language mismatch) but produces the identical downstream symptom - a direct second-order consequence of a gap WP-037 already flagged as unaddressed, not a new gap WP-038 introduced.
* **Manual target alignment remained high, confirming WP-037's improvement was preserved**: of the 10 accepted questions, 8 were manually judged genuinely aligned with their assigned concept (80%, in the same range as WP-037's 87.5%) - the two misalignments were both in `גרעיני הבסיס` (round 1 answered with a functional description rather than the named structure; round 2 answered about a different, sibling sub-structure, "Caudate Nucleus," entirely).
* **Zero false-positive identity matches were observed** (section 20E) - every `identity_recognized=True` case in the live data was a genuine same-concept exact match; confirmed independently by the dedicated "Safety" section of `tests/unit/test_concept_identity.py`.
* **Explicit architectural evaluation (WP-038 section 23 - required): Outcome C.** Safe, deterministic, evidence-derived cross-script identity is not feasible for the concepts this pilot actually encounters - not because the mechanism doesn't work (it is genuinely implemented and unit-tested working, when the evidence supports it), but because the pilot corpus does not contain the explicit-pairing pattern it safely requires. Per section 23's own required response: do not guess, preserve exact matching (done - normalized same-script matching is fully preserved and unchanged in behavior), document the limitation (done, here and in the completion report), and reconsider whether generation language should instead be constrained - this is the explicit recommendation carried into the next WP. See `implementation/WP-038_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data, safety analysis, and comparison tables against both WP-036 and WP-037.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-039)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-039) → **1304 passed**, up from 1286 (WP-038's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical (no public contract touched - `ConceptIdentity`/`InventoryConcept` are unmodified internal-only models).
* **Offline inventory analysis (section 19, before the live run)**: of 139 raw concepts across the three pilot categories, 12 trailing-truncation reconstruction attempts succeeded (11 after post-repair deduplication - `Corpos Striatum`, `Globus Pallidus Externus`, `Pars Reticulata`, `Internal`, `Putamen`, `Anterior Corticospinal Tract`, `Upper Motor Neuron`, `Neuromuscular Junction`, plus one residual limitation below), 2 were correctly excluded as ambiguous/malformed (`(GiP )`, `Substantia Nigra P`), and 115 needed no repair. `אספקת דם` had zero trailing-truncation cases. **One disclosed residual limitation**: `Deep Brain Stimulati` reconstructs to `Deep Brain Stimulatino D(SB.)` - structurally valid (balanced parens, unambiguous single direction) but wrong, because the single completing fragment line is itself internally letter-scrambled by an unusually severe extraction failure in that one passage - a form of corruption within a single fragment, not fragment-ordering, genuinely indistinguishable from a valid fragment by any purely structural check, and explicitly out of scope (section 11: no general text correction/OCR-error fixing).
* **Primary success criterion deterministically verified (section 22), independent of the live pilot's own natural randomness**: reproducing WP-038's own exact failure shape offline - `CategoryCoverage(tested_concepts=("Anterior Corticospinal Tract",))` against the post-repair inventory - confirms the concept is now correctly excluded, where it was not before WP-039 (stored as the truncated `"Anterior Corticospinal T"`). The same check for `"Corpos Striatum"` (formerly `"Corpos Str"`) also now excludes correctly.
* **Live pilot (section 20, one live test, no reruns)**: same three pilot categories, four sequential questions each, via `CategoryQuestionSetService`. **One clean attempt aborted before producing any data on a transient `httpx.ReadTimeout` network error** (zero rounds completed, nothing printed) - this is an infrastructure failure, not a result, so a second attempt was made per the same precedent WP-028 already established for an unrelated mid-run crash; the second attempt completed cleanly end to end and is the one reported below. **Accepted: `אספקת דם` 4/4, `גרעיני הבסיס` 3/4, `מסילות עצביות` 4/4 = 11/12** - matching WP-036's own 11/12 baseline exactly, a clear recovery from WP-037's 8/12 and WP-038's 10/12.
* **Extraction quality visibly improved in the live targets themselves**: `גרעיני הבסיס` and `מסילות עצביות` both assigned their concepts by clean, complete names (`Corpos Striatum`, `Anterior Corticospinal Tract`) rather than the truncated forms every prior WP was stuck showing (`Corpos Str`, `Anterior Corticospinal T`) - directly visible, qualitative confirmation of the extraction fix working in the exact live pipeline, not just in isolated tests.
* **Manual target alignment fell to ~45% (5/11)** - honestly reported, and honestly *not* attributed to this WP's own mechanism. Per-category breakdown: `אספקת דם` 1/4 (WP-039 made zero repairs here - three rounds drifted to a different, neighboring artery, ordinary generation stochasticity already documented as a background rate in WP-037/038); `גרעיני הבסיס` 1/3 excluding one failed round (twice answered with a functional description of `Corpos Striatum` rather than the structure's name - the *exact same* misalignment shape WP-037's own run already showed for this concept, present regardless of whether its stored text is complete or truncated); `מסילות עצביות` 3/4 (good, comparable to prior runs). Anchoring itself was never modified by this WP (confirmed: the full WP-037 anchoring test suite passes unmodified), so this is not an anchoring regression - it is ordinary run-to-run generation variance in a category (`אספקת דם`) this WP made zero changes to, plus one concept's already-known drift tendency.
* **Target selection rotation**: `אספקת דם` 2/4 distinct (rotated once, then stuck on `Basillar artery` for the same cross-script coverage-recognition reason WP-038 already disclosed as unaddressed and out of scope); `גרעיני הבסיס` 1/4 distinct (stuck on `Corpos Striatum` - no longer for a truncation reason, since the concept is now complete, but for the same functional-description-drift reason noted above); `מסילות עצביות` 3/4 distinct (`Spinothalamic Tract` -> `Medial Lemniscus Tract` -> `Anterior Corticospinal Tract`, its best rotation result across WP-036 through WP-039).
* **Comparison table (WP-036/037/038/039, same three categories):**

  | Metric | WP-036 | WP-037 | WP-038 | WP-039 |
  |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
  | Accepted count | 11/12 | 8/12 | 10/12 | **11/12** |
  | Manual target alignment | ~45% | 87.5% | 80% | ~45% (see above - not attributable to this WP) |
  | Known truncated concepts still assigned as targets | N/A | N/A | Yes (`Corpos Str`, `Anterior Corticospinal T`) | **No - both now complete** |
  | Primary-criterion offline verification | N/A | N/A | N/A | **Confirmed - concept now excluded once fully answered** |
  | False repairs observed | N/A | N/A | N/A | **1 disclosed (`Deep Brain Stimulatino D(SB.)`), did not reach the live pilot's selected targets** |

* **Explicit architectural evaluation (WP-039 section 25 - required): closest to Outcome A for the mechanism itself (successful, unambiguous repair of every real observed case except one disclosed residual corruption case; zero confirmed false repairs among live-selected targets; primary success criterion deterministically verified), but the *live pilot's* aggregate alignment number does not read as a clean win, because it is dominated by causes this WP explicitly does not touch.** The correct takeaway is not "alignment got worse because of WP-039" - it is "extraction is now reliably clean, and the categories' remaining alignment gap is fully attributable to the already-disclosed WP-037/038 boundaries (generation drift, cross-script coverage)." See `implementation/WP-039_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data, per-concept offline analysis table, and recommendation.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-040)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-040) → **1313 passed**, up from 1304 (WP-039's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical (`QuestionTarget` is never schema-exported - it is planning-internal, never part of `CategoryQuestionSetRequest`/`Response`/`ExamQuestion`).
* **Pre-change investigation (section 3, before any code was written)**: inspected the real generation prompt/contract directly. Found the existing prompt (`prompts/generation/question.txt`) already explicitly *permitted* narrowing "to one function" as an acceptable target-narrowing choice - directly explaining why a named-entity target like `Corpos Striatum` could legitimately be answered with a functional description under the pre-WP-040 contract. Found the concept inventory already carries everything needed to classify a pilot-category target as a named entity (it is one, by construction, the moment it passes `extract_concept_inventory()`'s own structural filter) - no new classifier, LLM or heuristic, was needed.
* **Offline evaluation (section 21, one real generation call each, no config changes after seeing individual outputs)**: `Corpos Striatum`→`Corpos Striatum`, `Medial Lemniscus Tract`→`Medial Lemniscus Tract`, `Superior cerebellar artery`→`עורק סופריור צרבלרי`, `Anterior Corticospinal Tract`→`מסילה קדמית של הקורטיקוספינלית` - **4/4 correct answers identified their target concept**, zero functional/property/related-entity substitutions.
* **Live pilot (section 22, one fresh run, no reruns)**: same three pilot categories, four sequential questions each, via `CategoryQuestionSetService`. **Accepted: `אספקת דם` 3/4, `גרעיני הבסיס` 4/4, `מסילות עצביות` 4/4 = 11/12** - matching WP-036/039's own 11/12 baseline.
* **Manual target alignment: 11/11 (100%) among accepted questions** - every single accepted question across all three categories correctly identified its assigned named-entity target; zero functional-description or related-entity substitutions anywhere in the run. `גרעיני הבסיס`'s `Corpos Striatum` was answered `"קורפוס סטריאטום"` (a genuine Hebrew transliteration identifying the target) in all four rounds - a complete reversal of WP-037's and WP-039's own repeated finding that this exact concept tends to drift toward a functional description ("involved in executing planned motor movements", "enables coordinated, planned movement").
* **This cleanly isolates the one remaining blocker, exactly as WP-039's own review predicted it would**: `גרעיני הבסיס` still rotated to only 1/4 distinct concepts - not because generation failed to identify the target (it succeeded all four rounds) but because WP-038's already-disclosed, deliberately-untouched cross-script coverage gap still cannot recognize a correct Hebrew answer against the English-stored concept `Corpos Striatum`. `אספקת דם` rotated 2/4 (same pattern - two rounds' Hebrew answers unrecognized, one round's English exact match correctly triggered rotation, matching WP-038's already-established boundary condition). `מסילות עצביות` rotated through **4/4 distinct concepts - its best rotation result across every pilot WP to date** (`Spinothalamic Tract` → `Medial Lemniscus Tract` → `Anterior Corticospinal Tract` → `Lateral Corticospinal Tract`, all four rounds answering in English and therefore all four correctly recognized by the unmodified WP-034/036 exact-match coverage path).
* **One disclosed secondary cost, honestly reported rather than omitted (section 25 "Attempts" criterion)**: average attempts-per-accepted-question rose from WP-039's ~1.09 to ~1.45 this run - driven specifically by `גרעיני הבסיס` (avg 2.25 attempts, vs. WP-039's own ~1.33 for the same category), while `אספקת דם` and `מסילות עצביות` both stayed at the ideal 1.0. Still comfortably within the existing 3-attempt budget, with no new exhaustion pattern beyond one ordinary `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError` (`אספקת דם` round 4) - not attributed with certainty to the new answer-identity requirement (a small-sample, single-run observation), but not swept under the rug either.
* **Explicit interpretation against WP-040 section 26's own success rule**: "higher alignment + stable acceptance + stable attempt budget + improved concept rotation" - **alignment improved dramatically (100% vs. WP-039's ~45%), acceptance stayed stable (11/12, unchanged), the attempt budget was not fully stable (see above, though not materially threatening), and rotation improved for one category while remaining blocked for two others by an already-disclosed, deliberately out-of-scope gap.** Per the same section: this is not judged a success "from acceptance count alone" - the alignment result is the primary, decisive evidence, independently confirmed twice (offline before the live run, and again live). See `implementation/WP-040_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data and comparison against WP-036 through WP-039.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-041)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-041) → **1325 passed**, up from 1313 (WP-040's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical (no `QuestionTarget`/public-contract change - the language decision is derived purely from existing fields, never a new one).
* **Pre-change investigation**: confirmed every pilot-category `QuestionTarget.topic` is, by construction, always pure-ASCII/English-representable (the same invariant WP-040's `named_entity_target` already relies on) - no bilingual-pairing search or new model was needed. Found and reconciled a real, pre-existing textual tension: the base generation prompt's own "must be written in Hebrew... do not write the answer choices in English" instruction directly conflicted with the new English-first requirement's own text - fixed with a precise, narrow carve-out (exactly two things: the correct answer choice, and any in-question reference to the target's own name), leaving the general Hebrew default for everything else unchanged. Confirmed no validator conflict: `QualityValidator`'s own Hebrew-language check already explicitly tolerates "Hebrew or standard English/Latin" terminology.
* **Offline evaluation (one real generation call each, no config changes between calls)**: `Corpos Striatum`, `Medial Lemniscus Tract`, `Superior cerebellar artery`, `Anterior Corticospinal Tract` - **4/4 correct answers were fully English (ASCII), exact matches to the target's own text**, specifically including both concepts WP-040's own live pilot had answered in Hebrew (`Corpos Striatum`, `Superior cerebellar artery`) - direct, decisive confirmation that the exact WP-040 regression case is fixed.
* **Live pilot (one fresh run, no reruns)**: same three pilot categories, four sequential questions each, via `CategoryQuestionSetService`. **Accepted: `אספקת דם` 3/4, `גרעיני הבסיס` 2/4, `מסילות עצביות` 4/4 = 9/12** - down from WP-040's 11/12.
* **Primary success criterion: 9/9 (100%) English-first compliance** - every accepted question where the target required English produced a fully English correct answer; zero Hebrew answers where English was required. Manual target alignment was also 9/9 (100%) - identical to the English-first count, since every accepted answer was an exact match to its target's own text.
* **Concept rotation genuinely improved for both previously cross-script-stuck categories, live, not just offline**: `אספקת דם` reached 3/4 distinct concepts (`Superior cerebellar artery` -> `Basillar artery` -> `Anterior Inferior Cerebellar Artery (AICA)`), up from WP-040's 2/4; `גרעיני הבסיס` reached 2/4 distinct (`Corpos Striatum` -> `Caudate Nucleus`), up from WP-040's 1/4 (stuck every round). This is direct, live confirmation of WP-041's core hypothesis: an English answer is recognized by the existing, unmodified WP-034/036 exact-match coverage mechanism, which then correctly excludes the concept and lets the next one be selected - no coverage change of any kind was needed.
* **However, acceptance fell to 9/12 (75%), a material regression from WP-040's 11/12 (91.7%) - reported honestly, not minimized, and does not meet WP-041's own secondary success criterion B ("do not materially degrade the WP-040 baseline").** Three rounds failed with the ordinary, pre-existing `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError` (`אספקת דם` round 2; `גרעיני הבסיס` rounds 1 and 2, both for `Corpos Striatum`) - the same failure mechanism every prior WP has occasionally observed, not a new generation-contract failure, and not obviously mis-attributable to a defect in the new language-selection logic itself (English-first compliance was perfect in every round that *did* succeed). The plausible, **not confirmed**, hypothesis: layering a sixth simultaneous constraint (English-first) on top of the five WP-040 and prior WPs already impose (grounding, MCQ structure, quality, category match, answer-identity) makes every individual attempt statistically harder to satisfy for a concept whose evidence is already known (since WP-035) to be less structurally clean - `Corpos Striatum`/`גרעיני הבסיס` specifically account for 2 of the 3 failures.
* **Explicit interpretation, per WP-041's own section 29 rule ("verify this happens because of the intended causal chain, not some other unrelated behavior")**: the causal chain is directly confirmed for every category - English-first selection to existing exact-match coverage to correct exclusion to next-concept selection, live, with zero new coverage logic. This is a genuine, decisive win on the primary criterion. It is **not**, however, an unqualified success by WP-041's own full rubric, because of the disclosed acceptance cost - this is reported as a real, mixed result, not reframed as a clean one. See `implementation/WP-041_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data, offline evaluation detail, and comparison against WP-036 through WP-040.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-042/WP-043)

* **WP-042 (diagnostic only, no production code, no doc update at the time)**: investigated WP-041's acceptance regression (11/12 → 9/12) directly at the validator level, via `QuestionProducer` called directly (the same production component `CategoryQuestionSetService` already uses one layer up, which discards per-attempt validator detail before it reaches the pilot script). **Finding: English-first was not the cause.** Zero of the examined rejected attempts cited language; `Corpos Striatum`'s `factual_focus` was found to be the bare string `"Corpos Striatum"` (zero context) since WP-039; `Basillar artery`'s evidence positions it as the source feeding a different, more salient artery (`Superior Cerebellar Artery`), and generation's "which artery supplies X" question shape was consistently grounding-rejected as a result. Both targets were already at or beyond the attempt-budget edge under WP-040, before English-first existed - confirmed from already-available WP-040 pilot data, no new generation needed for that comparison.
* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-043) → **1350 passed**, up from 1325 (WP-041's own baseline; WP-042 added no tests), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical.
* **WP-043 built two deterministic, individually-correct, thoroughly-tested mechanisms addressing WP-042's diagnosis** - a root-cause fix for `Corpos Striatum`'s empty evidence (a genuine line-lookup gap for trailing-reconstructed concepts, verified directly, not a vague "sparse evidence" claim) plus a conservative, paragraph-boundary-respecting broader fallback; and a narrow, deterministic source-role detector for `Basillar artery`'s evidence shape, surfaced into the generation prompt as an informational note. **Live pilot (one fresh run, no reruns): overall acceptance regressed further to 5/12 (41.7%), down from WP-041's 9/12 - `Corpos Striatum` 0/4 (down from WP-041's 2/4), `Basillar artery`-affected `אספקת דם` 1/4 (down from WP-041's 3/4), `מסילות עצביות` unaffected at 4/4.** This does not meet WP-043's own primary success criteria and is reported honestly, not minimized.
