* **Supplementary diagnostic captures (after the pilot, for observability only, mirroring WP-042's own methodology - never a rerun of the pilot itself) revealed two new, more specific failure modes than WP-042's diagnosis anticipated.** `Corpos Striatum`'s newly-recovered context (confirmed genuine, non-fabricated) turned out to be specifically an **enumeration-introduction sentence** ("basal nuclei contain several sub-structures") - combined with WP-040's mandatory single-entity answer requirement, this appears to actively encourage an ambiguous list-membership question shape (every listed member is equally "a part of" the whole), exactly what the generation prompt's own pre-existing enumeration-target guidance already warns against; all 3 diagnostic attempts failed this way, never for a language reason. `Basillar artery`'s evidence-role note was found not to reliably change generation's actual question-construction behavior - it continued predominantly asking "which artery supplies X" rather than the suggested source/origin framing; a supplementary capture's own attempt 2 succeeded with materially the same evidence, showing the blocker is generation's inconsistent adoption of the suggested framing rather than a hard architectural wall.
* **Explicit architectural evaluation (required, per WP-043's own section 26 fact-vs-inference labeling): both new mechanisms are individually correct and safe (confirmed via dedicated, thorough unit tests and direct empirical verification against the real corpus) - the negative pilot result does not indicate the underlying approach (deterministic evidence sufficiency; deterministic target-role signaling) is wrong in principle. Surfacing genuine evidence/relationship information turned out not to be sufficient, by itself, to make generation reliably construct a compliant question** - a materially more specific problem statement than WP-042 could have established before this WP's own live data existed. Target alignment (4/5 = 80%) and English-first compliance (5/5 = 100%) among accepted questions were both unaffected by the regression, confirming it is specifically an acceptance/reliability problem, not a language or identity-compliance one. See `implementation/WP-042_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` and `implementation/WP-043_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full diagnostic data, per-attempt validator reasoning, and comparison against WP-036 through WP-041.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-044)

* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-044) → **1379 passed**, up from 1350 (WP-043's own baseline), zero network access. `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three public schema files (`ExamRequest`/`ExamOutput`/`ExamAudit`) byte-identical - `QuestionTarget`'s two new fields (`is_enumeration_member`, `source_relationship_entity`) are internal-only, never schema-exported, the same precedent as every prior `QuestionTarget` field addition.
* **Part A (enumeration-shaped evidence) built as a deterministic skip, not a prompt-only mitigation.** `is_enumeration_evidence_insufficient()` (`planning/concept_anchor.py`) reuses the already-anchored `factual_focus` (no new retrieval): true only when a narrow enumeration-introduction cue phrase appears in the backward-anchored context **and** the forward-anchored context carries fewer than 2 alphabetic characters - exactly the real `Corpos Striatum` shape WP-043 diagnosed (shared list-intro sentence, then a bare bullet marker, nothing member-specific). `_plan_targets_from_concept_inventory()` now skips such a concept entirely, per WP-044 section 11's "prefer missing over wrong" - the same skip precedent WP-043's own insufficiency check already established. Directly verified against current real corpus retrieval (not merely the pilot's own behavior): `is_enumeration_evidence_insufficient()` returns `True` for `Corpos Striatum` today.
* **Part B (source-role evidence) given a concrete, named relationship, plus a deterministic safety net.** `extract_source_relationship_entity()` (`planning/target_role.py`) deterministically identifies the *other*, downstream entity a source-role target's evidence positions it as the source of, by scanning backward from the target's own line for the nearest candidate-concept heading line, skipping the intervening cue/bullet-marker lines - verified directly against the real `Basillar artery` corpus chunk: returns exactly `"Superior cerebellar artery"`. `QuestionTarget.source_relationship_entity` carries this into `format_target_evidence_role()`'s existing prompt section (naming the entity explicitly instead of only the generic role label) and into a new deterministic pre-validator check, `_validate_target_role_consistency()` (`generation/generator.py`) - rejects a candidate whose own correct-answer text names the downstream entity instead of the target, raising `InvalidGeneratedOutputError` exactly as the pre-existing provenance check already does (same attempt budget, no new retry loop, the five WP-013 validators completely untouched).
* **Live pilot (one fresh run, same three categories/four sequential questions each, `CategoryQuestionSetService`, no reruns): 8/12 accepted (66.7%), up from WP-043's 5/12 (41.7%) - a genuine, material improvement, meeting WP-044's own acceptance criterion without relaxing any validator or increasing the retry budget.** (The first attempt at this run was killed after 590s by an output-buffering/timeout artifact of the pilot script itself - `python3`'s stdout was fully block-buffered through a `| tail` pipe and the process was terminated before any line, or the output JSON file, was ever written, so zero data of any kind was produced; per this project's established precedent - a genuine infrastructure failure producing zero data is not a forbidden rerun - the run was restarted once, unmodified except for `python3 -u` and direct file redirection, and completed cleanly.)
* **`Corpos Striatum`: never selected in any of the four `גרעיני הבסיס` rounds** - a direct, complete reversal of WP-043's own finding (previously selected and rejected all 4 rounds). Confirmed both by the pilot's own live target-selection sequence and independently offline (see above) - the skip fires correctly on today's real corpus, not merely in a synthetic unit test.
* **`Basillar artery`: selected once, accepted on its second attempt.** Both attempts constructed a source/origin-framed question (`"איזה עורק הוא המקור לעורק הסופריור צ'רבלר"` - "which artery is the source of the superior cerebellar artery") - never once the previously-dominant "which artery supplies X" downstream framing WP-042/WP-043 diagnosed. Attempt 1 was rejected only for an unrelated reason (a second answer choice was also evidence-supported - an ordinary MCQ-ambiguity rejection, not a role-framing failure); attempt 2 passed all five validators cleanly. The new deterministic consistency check was never actually triggered this run - generation got the framing right on its own once given the concrete named relationship - verified correct by dedicated unit tests, not yet exercised by a live rejection (the same "safe but not yet production-proven" honesty WP-043 applied to its own unexercised broad-fallback path).
* **Target alignment: 8/8 (100%) among accepted questions** (deterministic preclassification: the correct answer's normalized text contains, or is contained in, the target's own normalized topic). **English-first compliance: 8/8 (100%) ASCII-English** correct answers. **Concept rotation**: `אספקת דם` 4/4 distinct targets (its best result to date); `גרעיני הבסיס` 3/4 distinct (`Caudate Nucleus`, `Nucleus Accumbens`, `Globus Pallidus` - the last repeated across rounds 3-4 since it was never accepted, so coverage never marked it tested, the same "coverage is not the blocker; the concept just never gets accepted" mechanism WP-043 already identified); `מסילות עצביות` 3/4 distinct (`Corticospinal Tract` similarly repeated rounds 3-4).
* **A new, more specific finding, disclosed rather than smoothed over: the same underlying "generic classification/membership ambiguity" failure shape recurred for two targets Part A's narrow detector does not cover.** `Globus Pallidus` (`גרעיני הבסיס`, rounds 3-4) and `Corticospinal Tract` (`מסילות עצביות`, rounds 3-4) each exhausted all 3 attempts, every rejection citing grounding's `other_answers_not_equally_correct=false` - multiple answer choices equally satisfied an overly-generic question (e.g. "which of the following is part of the basal nuclei"; "which motor tract is responsible for innervating skeletal muscles"). Neither target's own narrow anchored evidence happens to contain an enumeration-introduction cue phrase within its bounded backward-anchor window, so Part A's detector correctly does not fire for them (it was built for, and only ever fires on, the one demonstrated `Corpos Striatum` shape) - but the same underlying question-construction failure mode is evidently broader than that one narrow, deterministic signal can catch. This is not a defect in Part A (which behaves exactly as designed and tested), but an honest, more specific problem statement for the next WP: the general "testing enumeration or classification targets" prompt guidance alone still does not reliably prevent this question shape for targets outside Part A's narrow detection window. See `implementation/WP-044_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round/per-attempt data (every validator's pass/fail and reason, for every attempt, not just the final outcome) and comparison against WP-036 through WP-043.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-045, diagnostic only - no production code changed)

* **WP-045 investigated `Globus Pallidus`/`Corticospinal Tract` against the real corpus before writing any code, per its own "diagnosis first" mandate, and found they do NOT share a root cause.** `Corticospinal Tract`'s failure is a confirmed parent/child hierarchy ambiguity - every observed grounding rejection explicitly named a named child (`Anterior`/`Lateral Corticospinal Tract`) as also evidence-supported. `Globus Pallidus`'s dominant failure mode (6/8 observed rejections) is a broader classification ambiguity with **no evidence-shape property found that distinguishes it from its structurally-identical, reliably-successful siblings** (`Caudate Nucleus`, `Nucleus Accumbens`) - a genuine, thoroughly-investigated negative finding.
* **A candidate deterministic signal (`has_named_child` - true when another inventory concept contains the target's own text as a substring) was designed and tested directly against the real corpus - and rejected after finding a confirmed false positive.** It cleanly flags both diagnosed targets, but also flags `Inferior Cerebellar Artery (PICA)` - a target cleanly accepted in both WP-044's and WP-045's own pilots. Investigation confirmed `"Posterior inferior cerebellar artery (PICA)"` and `"Inferior Cerebellar Artery (PICA)"` are the *same* real artery, extracted twice because one occurrence's raw line is simply missing the leading word "Posterior" - not a genuine hierarchy. Three narrowing refinements (same-chunk-only matching; a closed Latin-qualifier vocabulary excluding directional adjectives) were each found to either miss the confirmed positive case or fail to exclude the confirmed false positive - both the true and false positives use the identical directional-adjective word class, so no vocabulary split can separate them.
* **An independent, materially-informative finding from WP-045's own fresh live pilot: both previously-100%-failing targets succeeded at least once this run, on the exact same, unmodified production code.** `Corticospinal Tract` round 3 exhausted all 3 attempts (identical failure shape to WP-044's own pilot); round 4, a structurally identical question, was accepted on attempt 1 - because the generator's own distractor choice that attempt happened not to include either named child, so grounding's own per-answer-choice ambiguity check never had reason to flag it, even though the underlying evidence ambiguity is equally present in both attempts. This demonstrates that whether the ambiguity is exposed as a live rejection depends substantially on the generator's own stochastic distractor selection, not solely on a fixed evidence-shape property - a second, independent reason (beyond the confirmed false positive) not to deploy any pre-generation, evidence-shape-only skip for this problem family: even a signal with zero false positives elsewhere would still have permanently blocked this pilot's own genuinely-successful `Corticospinal Tract` round 4.
* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-045) → **1379 passed**, unchanged from WP-044's own baseline (no code was modified). `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical.
* **Live pilot: 11/12 accepted (91.7%), up from WP-044's 8/12 - explicitly NOT claimed as a WP-045 improvement, since no code changed; this is disclosed as genuine run-to-run stochastic variance, consistent with the finding above.** `Globus Pallidus`'s round-3 acceptance came via a functional-description answer (`"מדכא את התלמוס ומפחית תנועה"` - "suppresses the thalamus and reduces movement," Hebrew, not the target's own name) - a live, disclosed instance of the already-known WP-040/043 "target identity is strongly instructed, not structurally guaranteed" weakness, not hidden behind the higher accepted count. Target alignment fell to 10/11 (90.9%, down from WP-044's 8/8) and English-first compliance to 10/11 (90.9%) for the identical single-instance reason - both explicitly disclosed as a real cost the higher acceptance number would otherwise obscure, not smoothed over. Concept rotation: `אספקת דם` 4/4 distinct (unchanged); `גרעיני הבסיס`/`מסילות עצביות` 3/4 distinct each.
* **Outcome C (WP-045 section 15): no safe deterministic signal was found for this ambiguity family; no production code was changed.** See `implementation/WP-045_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full diagnostic data, the three rejected refinement attempts, and the recommended WP-046 direction (a post-generation, distractor-text-containment check targeted at the confirmed `Corticospinal Tract` mechanism, rather than a pre-generation evidence-shape prediction).

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-046)

* **WP-046 built a deliberate case set from real production data across all three pilot categories before implementing anything** - known failures, successful controls (22 real accepted candidates), parent/child cases (both confirmed `TRUE_PARENT_CHILD` from the evidence's own explicit sub-list structure, never inferred from string shape alone), the mandatory `Inferior Cerebellar Artery (PICA)` false-positive control, and a classification-ambiguity family. **A new finding revised WP-045's own characterization**: `Caudate Nucleus` - previously cited as "reliably successful" - itself failed twice in WP-045's own pilot data with the identical generic basal-nuclei-membership framing that plagues `Globus Pallidus`, confirming this classification-ambiguity risk is target-independent, not `Globus Pallidus`-specific.
* **`_validate_distractor_containment()` (new, `generation/generator.py`) was implemented only after all ten of WP-046's own explicit safety criteria were checked and passed against the real case set** - a deterministic, post-generation pre-validator check (same category as WP-044's own `_validate_target_role_consistency`, not a sixth validator): for named-entity targets, rejects a candidate whose correct answer and any distractor's own text stand in a plain substring-containment relationship. 5 real true positives, 0 false positives (including the `PICA` control) across the full case set.
* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-046) → **1386 passed**, up from 1379 (WP-045's own baseline). `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical. Only `generation/generator.py` and its own tests were touched; the five validators, coverage, retry budget, English-first, and every WP-044 mechanism are byte-for-byte unchanged.
* **Live pilot (one fresh run, required since code changed): 11/12 accepted (91.7%) - the new check fired exactly 3 times, all for `Corticospinal Tract` round 3, each correctly identifying a real chosen distractor (`Lateral`/`Anterior Corticospinal Tract`) containing the correct answer's own text, consuming the attempt budget before any validator ran.** Zero false positives elsewhere across the full 12-round, 17-attempt run - including `Inferior Cerebellar Artery (PICA)`, again cleanly accepted.
* **A new, important finding, disclosed rather than hidden behind the 11/12 headline number: closing this one ambiguity pathway did not make `Corticospinal Tract` a fully safe target.** Round 4 was accepted, but its correct answer was `"Precentral Gyrus"` (the tract's own origin location) - not `"Corticospinal Tract"` itself - a live recurrence of the exact target-identity weakness WP-043's architecture review first flagged, now observed a second time, independently. The new check correctly does not fire (no containment relationship exists) since this is a structurally different problem. Target alignment for this pilot: **10/11 (90.9%)**, for this specific, newly-disclosed reason. English-first: **11/11 (100%)**. Concept rotation: `אספקת דם` 4/4, `גרעיני הבסיס` **4/4 (best result to date)** - a direct, positive consequence of `Globus Pallidus` finally being accepted with a correctly-aligned answer this run, letting coverage rotate to a new target; `מסילות עצביות` 3/4.
* **`Globus Pallidus` remains explicitly, deliberately unresolved** - no safe deterministic signal (candidate-level or otherwise) was found for its classification-ambiguity family, now confirmed to also affect `Caudate Nucleus`. See `implementation/WP-046_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full case set, generalization matrix, and the recommended WP-047 direction.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-047)

* **WP-047 searched this project's entire real historical dataset (54 real accepted pilot-category questions, WP-036 through WP-046) for a legitimate case where a named-entity target's correct answer intentionally identified a different entity - and found zero.** Every one of 7 real historical `answer != target` instances found was already independently diagnosed, at the time, as a genuine defect (a downstream-entity substitution, a functional description, an unrelated sibling entity, or the `Corticospinal Tract`/`Precentral Gyrus` shape, twice). This empirically stronger-than-expected result (a *general* contract, not merely a narrower relationship-specific one) is reached because the evidence supports it, not assumed.
* **`_validate_target_answer_identity()` (new, `generation/generator.py`), implemented only after all eleven of WP-047's own explicit safety criteria were checked and passed**: for named-entity targets, rejects a candidate whose correct-answer text does not contain the target's own topic text (normalized) - one-directional containment only (never the reverse, which would incorrectly accept a bare partial-word fragment). Generalizes WP-044 Part B's own narrow `is_source_role` pattern to every named-entity target. Validated safe specifically because WP-041's English-first policy already eliminates the legitimate-cross-script-answer scenario this exact check would otherwise have unsafely flagged (every historical Hebrew-transliteration accepted answer in the dataset predates WP-041); `ConceptIdentity` was investigated and found insufficient for this purpose (never recognizes a cross-script form in this corpus) and was not used.
* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-047) → **1396 passed**, up from 1386 (WP-046's own baseline). `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical. Only `generation/generator.py` and its own tests were touched; the five validators, coverage, retry budget, English-first, and WP-044/WP-046 mechanisms are byte-for-byte unchanged.
* **Live pilot (one fresh run, required since code changed): the new check fired zero times, directly verified by inspecting every one of 29 real attempts' own rejection message - yet all 7 accepted answers were independently confirmed target-identity-aligned (7/7 = 100%), the first pilot in this project's history with a *structural* guarantee behind that number.** English-first: 7/7 (100%).
* **The pilot's own raw acceptance fell to 7/12 (58.3%), down from WP-046's 11/12 - a real, disclosed number, verifiably NOT attributable to this WP's own change.** Directly traced instead to WP-046's own distractor-containment check (fired 5 times this run, up from 3, for `Corticospinal Tract`) and ordinary validator-level stochastic variance for two previously-100%-reliable targets (`Basillar artery` exhausted rounds 2-3; `Nucleus Accumbens` exhausted rounds 2-3) - both rejected for ordinary grounding/MCQ/textbook reasons unrelated to target identity. Concept rotation fell correspondingly: `אספקת דם` 2/4, `גרעיני הבסיס` 2/4, `מסילות עצביות` 3/4 - a direct consequence of lower acceptance, not a new mechanism.
* **A new, disclosed finding: `Nucleus Accumbens`'s own rejections this pilot showed the identical generic classification-ambiguity shape already found unresolved for `Globus Pallidus`/`Caudate Nucleus`** - a third real instance of that same family - **plus a related new shape observed for `Basillar artery` for the first time** (`"Both the Basilar artery and the Anterior Inferior Cerebellar Artery (AICA) are..."`). Neither was addressed by this WP, per its own explicit scope boundary (do not make classification ambiguity the primary implementation target). See `implementation/WP-047_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full target-to-answer matrix, relationship inventory, and safety analysis.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-048, diagnostic only - no production code changed)

* **WP-048 formally reconstructed every real classification-ambiguity failure (`Globus Pallidus`, `Caudate Nucleus`, `Nucleus Accumbens`, `Basillar artery` - 19 real rejected attempts total) and computed explicit P(x)-satisfaction tables against the authoritative evidence, from real production data already captured by WP-045/046/047's own pilots - no new live pilot was run (no code changed).**
* **The single most important finding: this is not a safety/detection gap.** `GroundingValidator`/`MCQValidator` already correctly reject every genuine instance of this family - e.g. for `P(x) = member of Corpus Striatum`: `true` for `Caudate Nucleus`/`Putamen`/`Nucleus Accumbens`, `false` for `Globus Pallidus`, a genuinely ambiguous predicate whenever the LLM chooses to test it, and grounding's own free-text reasoning already names exactly this, every time. This is a **reliability** problem (generation repeatedly attempts the same invalid question shape and exhausts the fixed 3-attempt budget) - a fundamentally different kind of issue than WP-044/046/047 each closed.
* **The one candidate signal that worked for a structurally similar prior problem (WP-046's `Corticospinal Tract` distractor-set differencing) was directly tested against real data and falsified**: the *same* four-entity candidate set was found in both a failing and a successful `Caudate Nucleus` round - the deciding factor is the generated question's own semantic framing (bare category membership vs. identity-by-name or a target-unique property), not which entities are chosen as distractors. No deterministic signal was found - **Outcome C for this subclass** (`QUESTION_PREDICATE`), reached only after directly testing and falsifying the one available candidate, not assumed.
* **`MULTIPLE_SUBCLASSES`, not one problem**: `Basillar artery`'s own repeated rejections were found, on direct re-reading of the real corpus evidence, to be a different, only partially understood shape - the evidence's own explicit `מקור:` labeling supports only `Basillar artery` as `Superior cerebellar artery`'s source, yet grounding repeatedly also credits `AICA` (a sibling branch sharing the same upstream parent). Recorded as a disclosed **inference**, not a confirmed validator defect - explicitly not investigated further (validator changes are out of this WP's scope and would need their own dedicated, live-diagnostic-backed investigation).
* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-048) → **1396 passed**, unchanged from WP-047's own baseline (no code was modified). `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical. See `implementation/WP-048_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full P(x) analysis, classification table, and the specific reasons each candidate signal was rejected.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-049)

* **WP-049 ran a controlled experiment: does strengthening generation guidance (never adding another detector) reduce classification-ambiguity failures for `Globus Pallidus`/`Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens`?** The smallest plausible, evidence-grounded change was made to `prompts/generation/question.txt`'s existing "Testing enumeration or classification targets" section only: one additional worked example drawn directly from the real basal-nuclei failure shape, plus one explicit self-check "escape hatch" instruction for exactly the gap the real baseline data showed (what to do when no target-unique property can be found, instead of silently reverting to bare category membership). No other prompt section and no source code was touched.
* **Baseline** (11 real rounds, mined from WP-045/046/047's own already-captured pilot data): 9/11 accepted (81.8%), 16 rejected attempts, 14/16 (87.5%) classification-ambiguity-shaped, only 2/9 (22.2%) accepted rounds succeeded on the first attempt. **Revised** (one fresh controlled pilot, no reruns): 3/4 accepted (75%), 3 rejected attempts, 3/3 (100%) classification-ambiguity-shaped, but **3/3 (100%) accepted rounds succeeded on the first attempt**.
* **A genuinely mixed result, reported as `INCONCLUSIVE` rather than spun either way.** The classification-ambiguity share of rejections did not improve, and overall acceptance was nominally lower - neither meaningful at this sample size (`n=4` rounds). But the first-attempt-success jump (22.2% → 100%) is a real, measured secondary signal, and the one real failing round's own attempt sequence showed the model genuinely trying specific-property predicates (source/origin, then decision-making function) before reverting to the discouraged bare-membership form only on its third and final attempt - direct evidence the revised guidance is being partially, not reliably, followed.
* `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` (WP-049) → **1396 passed**, unchanged (a prompt-text-only change). `scripts/generate_schemas.py` re-run: all three schema files byte-identical. Full pilot (all three categories): 9/12 accepted overall; target alignment 9/9 (100%); English-first 9/9 (100%); WP-046 fired 6 times (`Corticospinal Tract` only, not a primary target - unaffected, working correctly); WP-047 fired 0 times (unaffected, working correctly).
* **No further prompt iteration was attempted after this one inconclusive result**, per this WP's own explicit "a clean negative result is preferable to prompt overfitting" instruction. The revised prompt was left in place since it regressed nothing measured and shows a real, if unconfirmed, positive signal - a properly-powered multi-pilot experiment (explicitly out of this WP's own API-cost-disciplined scope) would be needed to confirm or refute it. See `implementation/WP-049_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full baseline/revised comparison table, the exact prompt diff, and real generated-question examples from both conditions.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-050, diagnostic only - no production code changed)

* **WP-050 investigated whether the existing candidate/evidence architecture already contains, or could safely expose, structured `TRUE`/`FALSE`/`UNKNOWN` uniqueness information for `Globus Pallidus`/`Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens`, per WP-049's architecture review's own "more specific ≠ uniquely identifying" question - no new live pilot was run (architectural investigation, per its own "no fresh large pilot required" instruction); a prototype-only, zero-LLM-call inspection script executed the real, unmodified pipeline against real retrieved evidence instead.**
* **Confirmed by direct execution, not inferred: today's real generation prompt payload for all three primary targets carries zero deterministic distinguishing signal.** Each target's own anchored `factual_focus` is near-empty enumeration bullet noise (e.g. `Caudate Nucleus`'s: `"o\nCaudate Nucleus\no"`), `extract_relationship()` classifies every one `UNSPECIFIED` (no keyword match in that sparse text), and `discover_competitors()` consequently returns zero candidates for all three, every time. Whatever distinguishing reasoning occurs today happens entirely inside the LLM's free reading of the full, unstructured evidence blob it separately and unconditionally receives - there is no deterministic scaffolding at all for these targets.
* **A full-corpus, evidence-grounded `TRUE`/`FALSE`/`UNKNOWN` uniqueness matrix, built from direct reading of all 8 retrieved chunks, found a genuinely differentiated result per target.** `Globus Pallidus` has a real, unique, evidence-supported property (thalamus suppression via GPi in the direct pathway - never stated of the other three anywhere in the corpus), already reachable today via the full evidence text (matching its own comparatively higher real historical acceptance rate). `Caudate Nucleus` and `Nucleus Accumbens` have **no evidence-supported distinguishing fact anywhere in the retrieved corpus** - every real mention treats the three Striatum members as one undifferentiated group. Across all 16 real historical rounds mined (WP-045/046/047/049), every single accepted `Caudate Nucleus` round used a bare-identity/naming shape, never a property - there is no real precedent of a property-based question succeeding for this target.
* **Every candidate mechanism was rejected for a specific, evidence-based reason, not by default caution**: a deterministic property-extraction matrix would require semantic/NLP-adjacent parsing (the same risk shape as the already-forbidden semantic post-generation parser, just moved earlier in the pipeline); extending the existing relationship-keyword vocabulary (a safe, pure-data change) would help only `Globus-Pallidus`-shaped cases, not the two targets that actually need a solution; an external ontology was out of scope by design. **Explicit decision: Option C - the existing architecture cannot safely establish uniqueness for `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens`; do not implement; document the limit.** No production code was changed; `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` unaffected at **1396 passed** (no code touched, so no full regression is required per this WP's own instruction). See `implementation/WP-050_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full architecture inventory, candidate uniqueness matrix, option comparison, and false-positive/false-negative analysis.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-051, diagnostic only - no production code changed)

* **WP-051 investigated a narrower follow-up question from WP-050's own architecture review: not "does a unique property exist" but "can the existing evidence tell us, before generation, whether a target is worth attempting at all?" - no new live pilot was run (architectural investigation, existing pilot data plus two prototype-only, zero-LLM-call inspection scripts instead).**
* **Six of the seven candidate sufficiency signals named in the WP-051 spec, computed with existing production functions, plus one new full-evidence keyword-proximity scan (reusing the existing relationship-keyword vocabulary), were tested by direct execution and found to have zero discriminating power between `Globus Pallidus`/`Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens`** - every one is computed from the target's own narrow-or-broad `factual_focus`, and `Globus Pallidus`'s real distinguishing fact (WP-050) is never inside its own anchor either. **One new signal - counting how many of the 8 retrieved chunks mention the concept's own full name - correctly separates the three targets exactly as WP-050's hand-built ground truth does** (`Globus Pallidus`: 5 chunks; `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens`/`Putamen`: 2 each) and passes the required critical test (does not wrongly flag `Globus Pallidus` insufficient).
* **The decisive finding is about what using this signal as a target-skip mechanism would actually do, not about its accuracy**: of all 11 real historical `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens` rounds (WP-045/046/047/049), **8 (72.7%) were legitimately accepted** - every one via the bare-identity/naming question shape, which needs no distinguishing property, only the target's own name. A pre-generation skip based on this signal would have discarded all 8 of those real, valid, accepted questions to save only 9 wasted attempts across the 3 rounds that failed - a clearly unfavorable trade. **Explicit decision: Option C - do not implement target-selection filtering**, not because no signal was found, but because the one signal found answers a narrower question than the one target selection actually needs answered, and the existing 3-attempt retry budget's bare-identity fallback already reliably covers the gap. No production code was changed; `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` unaffected at **1396 passed**. See `implementation/WP-051_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full signal matrix, historical would-we-have-skipped-it analysis, and false-negative reasoning.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-052, diagnostic only - no production code changed)

* **WP-052 individually classified every one of 31 real historical generation attempts across all 15 real `גרעיני הבסיס` rounds (WP-045/046/047/049) as `PROPERTY` or `IDENTITY`, by a small deterministic keyword rule over the real generated question text - no new live pilot was run.**
* **The per-attempt breakdown is the starkest finding in this whole investigation chain**: `Caudate Nucleus` property attempts succeeded **0/8 (0%)**, identity attempts **4/4 (100%)**; `Nucleus Accumbens` property attempts 1/8 (12.5%, and that one success is the same evidentially-uncertain reward-system case WP-050 already flagged), identity 3/3 (100%); `Globus Pallidus` (the one target with a real, confirmed unique property) property 1/4 (25%), identity 3/4 (75%) - property questions remain genuinely viable there, unlike the other two.
* **8 wasted property attempts were directly observed (non-hypothetical) as recoverable** - rounds that eventually reached and succeeded via identity within the same round would have succeeded immediately had identity been attempted first, with zero change to accepted output. A further, explicitly-labeled hypothesis (not proven): the 3 fully-exhausted rounds that never reached identity within-round might also have succeeded had identity been tried first - up to 9 more attempts and 3 more accepted rounds as unconfirmed upside, flagged as the key open question for a future experiment.
* **The one deterministic, automatable signal available (WP-051's distinct-chunk count) is accurate on this sample but unvalidated at scale; the one signal that is actually accurate (WP-050's manual, exhaustive evidence read) is not automatable without forbidden semantic extraction or forbidden per-target hard-coding.** **Explicit decision: Option B - a conservative signal exists, but production implementation is not yet justified; recommend a narrow, controlled, prototype-only generation experiment before any permanent change** - the retrospective 100% identity success rate was observed only in rounds where identity was tried after property already failed, not under a genuine "identity first" instruction. No production code was changed; `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` unaffected at **1396 passed**. See `implementation/WP-052_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-attempt classification, baseline metrics, and historical counterfactual replay.

## Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-053, controlled experiment - no permanent production code changed)

* **WP-053 ran one real, controlled, fresh live experiment against the actual OpenAI API to answer the causal question WP-052 could not: does explicitly instructing generation to attempt an identity-based question first, for `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens` only, actually work - not just eventually, but immediately?** The experimental instruction was built entirely from existing injection points (`PromptRepository`'s explicit-mapping constructor, `QuestionGenerator`'s explicit `prompt_repository` parameter) as an in-memory-only appended block, never written to `prompts/generation/question.txt`; `Globus Pallidus` always used the genuinely unmodified control repository - a structural guarantee, not merely an observed behavior.
* **Both experimental rounds succeeded immediately, on attempt 1, with clean identity-shaped questions - 100% first-attempt acceptance, 100% strategy compliance, both independently and cleanly grounded, neither touching the disputed category-level reward-system fact.** A fresh current-control round (unmodified prompt, run the same day) showed the real contrast: `Caudate Nucleus` exhausted all 3 attempts on bare-membership property questions and was rejected entirely; `Nucleus Accumbens` succeeded via a hybrid identity+membership phrasing not previously seen in this project's data. The safety-check `Globus Pallidus` control round reproduced its own known pattern exactly (property questions failing on bare membership, then succeeding via a genuine, newly-observed location-based property fact) - confirmed unaffected, both structurally and behaviorally.
* **Explicit decision: Option A - the experiment strongly supports identity-first for these two targets specifically, with the small-sample caveat (n=2 rounds/condition) stated prominently - its strength is as a clean, uncontradicted causal confirmation of WP-052's much larger retrospective sample, not its own size alone.** No production prompt or code file was changed; `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` unaffected at **1396 passed**; schemas re-generated and confirmed byte-identical. **Recommend WP-054: a minimal, narrowly-scoped permanent identity-first preference for `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens` only - never a general rule, never applied to `Globus Pallidus` or any other target without further study.** See `implementation/WP-053_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full experiment record, per-attempt validator results, and decision criteria.

## Known Issues / Open Questions

* **Python version deviation from WP-001 spec (carried forward).** WP-001.md specified Python 3.14 and assumed a pre-existing project-local `.venv` built with it. Neither a Python 3.14 interpreter nor a pre-existing `.venv` was actually present on this machine (only system `python3` / `python3.12` = 3.12.3, confirmed via `apt list --installed` and filesystem search). Per explicit user instruction, WP-001 through WP-007 were implemented and verified against **Python 3.12.3** instead, with `pyproject.toml` declaring `requires-python = ">=3.12"`. This is a factual correction of the WP's environment assumption, not an architectural change. `openpyxl`, `pymupdf`, `scikit-learn`, and now `openai` were all confirmed to install and work correctly under 3.12.3.
* See "Known Retrieval-Quality Observations" above for WP-006-specific findings (all explicitly non-blocking per the WP's own acceptance criteria - honest reporting was required, not resolution).
* No open issues from WP-007. Live OpenAI compatibility (`gpt-4o-mini` + Responses API + `temperature` + structured output) was verified end-to-end against the real API by the user after this WP's initial completion report - see Live Smoke Test above.
* No open issues from WP-008. The real course-book retrieval index returned zero results for the specific canonical-category text queried during the real-data smoke test (a known, previously-documented WP-006 short-query ranking characteristic, not a WP-008 defect); the smoke test used a real course-book chunk taken directly from the real corpus instead so `TEXTBOOK_VALIDATION` was still exercised against genuine course-book content.
* No open issues from WP-009. The live generation smoke test (initially skipped - no key available at implementation time) was run successfully once the user supplied `OPENAI_API_KEY` - see "Live Generation Smoke Test (WP-009)" above for the generated question/answers.
* No open issues from WP-010. The live smoke test initially exposed a real, reproducible provenance-check failure caused by prompt wording (see "Decisions Made (WP-010)"); it was fixed (with explicit user approval, since it meant editing already-shipped WP-008 prompt files) and confirmed resolved by a subsequent successful live call. Only one real grounding verdict has been observed so far (`passed=True`, high confidence) - this is not a quality/accuracy measurement across multiple questions or categories, just a confirmation that the pipeline works end-to-end. WP-013 (diversity/retry) and broader manual review will be where real grounding-quality patterns get surfaced.
* No open issues from WP-011. The live smoke test's candidate happened to fail all four independent validators at once (Grounding/MCQ/Category/Quality) - this is an honest, self-consistent negative result about that one generated candidate (all four reasons independently converge on the same underlying weakness: the correct answer isn't the only defensible one, and the question is really about general vertebral function, not the requested category), not a validator defect, and per WP-011 section 15 is an explicitly acceptable, informative smoke-test outcome. No prompt was tuned in response. Only one real four-way verdict combination has been observed so far - not a systematic quality measurement across multiple candidates/categories.
* No open issues from WP-012. The live smoke test's textbook verdict was `NOT_FOUND` despite genuine (non-empty, 3-chunk) course-book retrieval - the LLM judged the retrieved material insufficient to confidently support or contradict the specific candidate, which is a legitimate `NOT_FOUND` use case distinct from the empty-retrieval short-circuit path (both were exercised: the empty-retrieval path in unit tests, the LLM-judged-insufficient path live). This contrasts with WP-008's own real-data smoke test, which saw zero course-book retrieval results for a bare category-name query (a documented WP-006 short-query ranking characteristic); WP-012's query additionally includes the full question and answer text, which was sufficient to retrieve real candidates in the live test. Only one real textbook verdict has been observed so far - not a systematic quality measurement across multiple candidates/categories.
* No open implementation issues from WP-013, but the live smoke test surfaced two real, worth-recording observations. First, a genuine prompt-contract defect in `prompts/validation/textbook.txt` (see "Decisions Made (WP-013)" - fixed with explicit user approval). Second, an honest quality-baseline data point: the one live production cycle exhausted all 3 attempts without an accepted candidate - Grounding passed all three times, but MCQ and/or Quality failed on all three, reinforcing (not merely repeating) the same generation-construction weaknesses WP-011's independent live smoke test already observed. This is not a WP-013 defect - it is exactly the honest baseline measurement WP-013 section 15 explicitly asks for ("This also gives us an honest baseline for how often repeated independent model generations pass"). Only one real production-cycle outcome has been observed so far - not a systematic acceptance-rate measurement across multiple categories/runs; sophisticated validator-feedback regeneration (which might improve this rate) is explicitly deferred, per the WP's own scope.
* **No open implementation issues from WP-014's own orchestration logic** - every live failure encountered during smoke testing propagated correctly and immediately, exactly as designed, from components several layers below `ExamOrchestrator`. Two distinct operational-reliability issues were surfaced and are recorded here rather than chased further, per this project's established "record honestly rather than tune prompts to force a pass" philosophy:
  * **`TextbookValidator`'s `reference_text` verbatim-quoting instruction is not 100%-reliable even after two prompt fixes.** WP-013 fixed a clear paraphrasing defect; WP-014 fixed a clear empty-string-vs-null ambiguity in that same fix's own wording (see "Decisions Made (WP-014)"). After both fixes, a small number of live calls (across several different real categories, both before and after the second fix) still hit `InvalidTextbookOutputError` on a `reference_text` that did not verbatim-match its cited evidence. A live investigation specifically ruled out a plausible *implementation* bug (Unicode-normalization false-mismatch in the plain-substring provenance check) - repeated debug calls against fixed real evidence produced either a clean `NOT_FOUND` with no citation, or a citation that was a genuine semantic paraphrase. This looks like an inherent `gpt-4o-mini` reliability ceiling for this specific instruction, not a wording defect with an obvious further fix - the correct system response (which is exactly what happens today) is to reject the unverifiable citation as an operational failure, not to weaken the provenance check.
  * **The WP-009 generator's evidence-id provenance check (fixed once already in WP-010) was seen to reject an invented `evidence_chunk_ids` value once more, live, for a different category** than WP-010's original finding. This is the same failure *class*, recurring for different specific content - not investigated further as a distinct new wording defect, since (like the textbook case above) it reflects the same class of imperfect, non-zero model non-compliance that these provenance checks exist specifically to catch rather than eliminate.
  * Neither issue blocks correct operation - both are correctly-propagated operational failures, exactly matching this project's designed fail-closed behavior throughout the validation/production/orchestration stack. A future WP could reasonably consider an explicit *operational*-failure retry policy (distinct from WP-013's quality-regeneration and WP-014's duplicate-replacement bounds, both of which are deliberately quality-only per their own explicit scope), if live reliability at this rate is judged unacceptable for production use - not attempted here, since WP-014 section 21 explicitly excludes "global API retry."
  * One clean, complete, first-attempt-success 2-question exam was still produced live (see "Live Exam Orchestration Smoke Test (WP-014)") - these reliability observations describe the frequency of a known failure mode across ~10 live calls made during this WP's testing, not a claim that the system cannot succeed.
* **No open issues from WP-015.** The one real-data smoke test succeeded cleanly on the first attempt (no operational failures this time), and the deterministic transformation/serialization logic is fully covered offline. Two known, deliberate scope gaps are documented rather than hidden: `historical_reference_id`/`evidence` are honestly represented as absent (not derivable without broader refactoring of already-shipped WP-009/WP-010/WP-012 components - see "Decisions Made (WP-015)"), and prompt-version/hash provenance was investigated and found genuinely unavailable, with no existing contract field requiring it. Neither is a defect in WP-015's own logic; both are candidates for a future WP if richer audit provenance is ever prioritized.
* **No open issues from WP-016.** The one real CLI smoke test succeeded cleanly (exit 0, both outputs correct) on the first attempt - no repeated invocations were needed this time, unlike several earlier WPs' live testing. One genuinely useful real-data observation surfaced (not a defect): the smoke test's single requested question needed 2 internal `QuestionProducer` attempts (one rejected, one accepted) before the CLI could report success, directly demonstrating live that the CLI's "only write output once the whole pipeline has fully succeeded" behavior and WP-015's full-attempt-history preservation both work correctly together, not just in mocked tests. During implementation, an early test-isolation bug caused two files (`exam.json`, `exam_audit.json`) to be briefly created in the real project repository root by a not-yet-isolated CLI test run; caught and deleted immediately, never committed, and fixed permanently with an autouse `_isolated_cwd` fixture applied to the whole test module (see "Decisions Made (WP-016)") - noted here for transparency, not because any trace of it remains.
* **The most significant finding of the whole project so far surfaced in WP-017**: a very high (45% of all planned questions across two independent live runs) empty-required-string-field structured-output failure rate, concentrated on `QualityValidationResult.reason` specifically, at `validation_temperature=0.0`. This is not a new failure *class* (the same class was noted occasionally in WP-013/WP-014), but its frequency here is much higher than any prior scattered observation - see "Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-017)" above for full data and "Decisions Made (WP-017)" for the two narrow instrumentation fixes (catching `pydantic.ValidationError`, capturing accepted-candidate text) this WP made to obtain a valid measurement of it. **No production/prompt tuning was performed in response to this finding**, per WP-017's own explicit "measure before improving" rule - this is exactly the kind of evidence WP-017 exists to surface for WP-018 to act on, not a WP-017 defect. The CLI's own error classification (`exam_generator/cli.py::_classify_error()`, WP-016) does not currently have a specific branch for a raw `pydantic.ValidationError` reaching it from deep in the pipeline (as opposed to the request-parsing case, which is already handled via `CliUsageError`) - it would currently propagate as an "unexpected" traceback rather than a clean `EXIT_GENERATION_FAILURE` message; not fixed here (WP-016 is already-shipped, out of scope for this WP's narrow instrumentation-only fixes), but worth WP-018 considering alongside the underlying reliability fix.
* **RESOLVED in WP-018: the empty-`reason` failure class above.** Root-caused (missing `Field(description=...)` on all five validation-result models, confirmed by direct reproduction) and fixed (schema descriptions + explicit prompt wording, applied to all five models/four prompts, not just `QualityValidationResult`/`quality.txt`). Zero occurrences in a 25-attempt post-hardening live evaluation and a separate 4-question final CLI run - see "Live Evaluation Baseline (WP-018)" above. The `cli.py::_classify_error()` gap noted above (no specific branch for a raw `pydantic.ValidationError`) was not fixed, since the underlying condition that would trigger it is now expected to be rare; still worth a future WP's attention if it recurs.
* **Known V1 limitation (WP-018, not resolved, not blocking)**: a residual, much-lower-rate provenance-invention failure mode remains - invented `evidence_chunk_ids` in generation/grounding/textbook validation occasionally still occurs (1 of 25 attempts in WP-018's post-hardening evaluation, versus ~20% combined in WP-017's baseline). The existing strict provenance checks continue to correctly reject it (fail closed, never silently accepting fabricated evidence) - this remains an inherent `gpt-4o-mini` reliability characteristic for exact-identifier-copying tasks, consistent with WP-014's own prior finding about `reference_text` verbatim-quoting reliability (itself since addressed for textbook specifically by WP-018's chunk-id redesign, but not for generation/grounding, where the underlying task - copying a supplied identifier exactly - was already the more robust mechanism and simply isn't 100% reliable). A future WP could reasonably consider a bounded, operational-failure-specific retry policy (distinct from WP-013's quality-regeneration bounds) if this rate is judged unacceptable for production use at larger scale - not implemented here, per WP-018's own explicit "no generic automatic LLM retry" scope exclusion.
