Every layer through `src/exam_generator/cli` exists and has been confirmed working end-to-end. The single most important things for the next WP's author to know:

* **WP-053 ran the real, controlled, live-API experiment WP-052 could not: does an explicit identity-first instruction, applied only to `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens`, causally improve first-attempt acceptance?** The experimental instruction was built entirely from existing injection points (`PromptRepository`'s explicit-mapping constructor, `QuestionGenerator`'s explicit `prompt_repository` parameter) as an in-memory-only appended block - never written to `prompts/generation/question.txt` - and used only for the two experimental targets; `Globus Pallidus` always used the genuinely unmodified control repository, a structural guarantee. **Both experimental rounds succeeded immediately on attempt 1** with clean identity-shaped questions (100% first-attempt acceptance, 100% strategy compliance, both cleanly grounded, neither touching the disputed category-level reward-system fact). **A fresh current-control round run the same day showed the real contrast**: `Caudate Nucleus` exhausted all 3 attempts on bare-membership property questions and was rejected entirely; `Nucleus Accumbens` succeeded via a hybrid identity+membership phrasing not previously seen in this project's data (a genuine new observation, disclosed explicitly, not hidden). **The safety-check `Globus Pallidus` control round reproduced its own known pattern exactly** (property questions failing on bare membership, then succeeding via a genuine, newly-observed location-based property fact from the corpus's own diagram description) - confirmed unaffected. **Explicit decision: Option A - the experiment strongly supports identity-first for these two targets, with the small-sample caveat (n=2 rounds/condition) stated prominently: its strength is as a clean, uncontradicted causal confirmation of WP-052's much larger retrospective sample, not its own size alone.** No production prompt or code file was changed. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: WP-054 should implement a minimal, narrowly-scoped permanent identity-first preference for `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens` specifically - never a general rule for other targets/categories, never applied to `Globus Pallidus`, and not assumed safe by extrapolation beyond what this experiment actually tested.** See `implementation/WP-053_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full experiment record, per-attempt validator results, and decision criteria.

* **WP-052 (diagnostic only, no production code) individually classified all 31 real historical generation attempts across the 15 real `גרעיני הבסיס` rounds (WP-045/046/047/049) as `PROPERTY` or `IDENTITY`, using a small deterministic keyword rule over the real generated question text - the most granular analysis in this investigation chain so far.** The per-attempt success-rate breakdown is the starkest, most corroborating finding yet: `Caudate Nucleus` property attempts succeeded **0/8 (0%)**, identity attempts **4/4 (100%)**; `Nucleus Accumbens` property 1/8 (12.5%, and that one success is the same evidentially-uncertain reward-system case WP-050 already flagged), identity 3/3 (100%); `Globus Pallidus` (the one target with a real, confirmed unique property) property 1/4 (25%), identity 3/4 (75%) - property questions remain genuinely viable only there. **8 wasted property attempts were directly observed (non-hypothetical) as recoverable** - rounds that eventually reached and succeeded via identity within the same round would have succeeded immediately had identity been attempted first, with zero change to accepted output; a further, explicitly-labeled hypothesis (not proven) suggests up to 9 more attempts and 3 more accepted rounds as unconfirmed upside for the 3 fully-exhausted rounds that never reached identity at all. **The real limiting factor: the one accurate signal (WP-050's manual, exhaustive evidence read) is not automatable without forbidden semantic extraction or per-target hard-coding, while the one automatable signal (WP-051's chunk count) is accurate here but unvalidated at scale.** **Explicit decision: Option B - a conservative signal exists, but production implementation is not yet justified.** **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: the retrospective 100% identity success rate was observed only in rounds where identity was tried after property already failed, not under a genuine "identity first" instruction from attempt 1 - WP-053 should run one narrow, controlled, prototype-only generation experiment (never production, mirroring WP-049's own one-shot-no-reruns discipline) instructing generation to attempt identity-first for `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens` specifically (never `Globus Pallidus`, whose property strategy must be preserved), and measure whether the effect actually holds.** See `implementation/WP-052_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-attempt classification, baseline metrics, and historical counterfactual replay.

* **WP-051 (diagnostic only, no production code) investigated whether the existing evidence can tell us, before generation, whether a target is worth attempting at all - narrower than WP-050's own "does a unique property exist" question.** Six of the seven candidate sufficiency signals named in its spec (computed from existing production functions) plus one new full-evidence keyword-proximity scan were tested by direct execution and found to have zero discriminating power between `Globus Pallidus`/`Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens` - all are computed from the target's own anchor, and `Globus Pallidus`'s real distinguishing fact is never inside its own anchor either. **One new signal - counting how many of the 8 retrieved chunks mention the concept's own full name - correctly separates the three targets** (`Globus Pallidus`: 5; `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens`/`Putamen`: 2 each) and passes the required critical test of not wrongly flagging `Globus Pallidus`. **The decisive finding is about what using this signal as a skip mechanism would do, not its accuracy**: of 11 real historical `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens` rounds, 8 (72.7%) were legitimately accepted via the bare-identity/naming shape (no distinguishing property needed) - a pre-generation skip would have discarded all 8 to save only 9 wasted attempts across the 3 failing rounds, a clearly unfavorable trade. **Explicit decision: Option C - do not implement target-selection filtering.** **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: a narrower, more promising, unexplored direction is whether generation itself could be told, deterministically, to skip straight to a bare-identity question for a target known to lack a distinguishing property, rather than skip the target entirely - this would touch the generation prompt and was explicitly out of this WP's own scope.** See `implementation/WP-051_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full signal matrix, historical would-we-have-skipped-it analysis, and false-negative reasoning.

* **WP-050 (diagnostic only, no production code) directly executed the real, unmodified pipeline against real retrieved evidence for `גרעיני הבסיס` and found today's generation prompt payload for all three primary targets carries zero deterministic distinguishing signal** - each target's own anchored `factual_focus` is near-empty enumeration bullet noise, `extract_relationship()` classifies every one `UNSPECIFIED`, and `discover_competitors()` returns zero candidates for all three, confirmed by direct execution rather than inferred. A full-corpus `TRUE`/`FALSE`/`UNKNOWN` uniqueness matrix, built from reading all 8 retrieved chunks directly, found `Globus Pallidus` has a genuine unique property (thalamus suppression via GPi) already reachable through the full evidence text generation already receives - while `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens` have **no evidence-supported distinguishing fact anywhere in the corpus**; every real mention treats the three Striatum members as one undifferentiated group, and across all 16 real historical rounds mined, every accepted `Caudate Nucleus` round used a bare-identity shape, never a property. Every candidate mechanism (a deterministic property matrix, an extended relationship-keyword vocabulary, an external ontology) was evaluated and rejected or scoped down for a specific, evidence-based reason - not by default caution. **Explicit decision: Option C - the existing architecture cannot safely establish uniqueness for `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens`; do not implement; document the limit.** **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: this is now confirmed to be an evidence-content limit for two of the three targets, not a representation or reliability gap - no further architecture work on candidate uniqueness is justified for `Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens` without new source material; `Basillar artery`'s own separate WP-048 thread and the open `Nucleus Accumbens` "reward system" validator-fidelity question both remain untouched.** See `implementation/WP-050_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full architecture inventory, candidate uniqueness matrix, option comparison, and false-positive/false-negative analysis.

* **WP-049 ran WP-048's own recommended next step: a controlled experiment testing whether strengthened generation guidance (never another detector) reduces classification-ambiguity failures for `Globus Pallidus`/`Caudate Nucleus`/`Nucleus Accumbens`.** The smallest plausible, evidence-grounded change was made to `prompts/generation/question.txt`'s existing "Testing enumeration or classification targets" section only - one additional worked example drawn directly from the real basal-nuclei failure shape, plus one explicit self-check "escape hatch" for exactly the gap the real data showed (what to do when no target-unique property can be found, instead of silently reverting to bare category membership). No other prompt section and no source code was touched. **Baseline** (11 real rounds, mined from WP-045/046/047's own data): 9/11 accepted (81.8%), 14/16 (87.5%) of rejections classification-ambiguity-shaped, only 2/9 (22.2%) accepted rounds succeeded on the first attempt. **Revised** (one fresh controlled pilot, no reruns): 3/4 accepted (75%), 3/3 (100%) of rejections classification-ambiguity-shaped, but **3/3 (100%) accepted rounds succeeded on the first attempt**. **A genuinely mixed result, honestly reported as `INCONCLUSIVE`** rather than spun either way: no improvement in the classification-ambiguity share of rejections, and nominally lower overall acceptance - neither meaningful at `n=4` rounds - but a real, measured first-attempt-success jump, and direct qualitative evidence (the one failing round's own attempt sequence tried a source/origin predicate, then a decision-making-function predicate, before reverting to the discouraged bare-membership form only on its third and final attempt) that the revised guidance is being partially, not reliably, followed. Per this WP's own explicit "a clean negative result is preferable to prompt overfitting" instruction, **no further prompt iteration was attempted** - the revised prompt was left in place (it regressed nothing measured: WP-044/046/047, English-first, target alignment, schemas, validators, and the retry budget all confirmed intact) since it shows a real, if unconfirmed, positive signal a properly-powered multi-pilot experiment (explicitly out of this WP's own API-cost-disciplined scope) would be needed to confirm or refute. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: do not iterate further on this exact prompt wording without a properly-powered multi-pilot experiment design; `Basillar artery`'s own separate, still-unconfirmed grounding-interpretation finding (WP-048) remains open and untouched.** See `implementation/WP-049_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full baseline/revised comparison table, the exact prompt diff, and real generated-question examples from both conditions.

* **WP-048 (diagnostic only, no production code) formally reconstructed every real classification-ambiguity failure (`Globus Pallidus`, `Caudate Nucleus`, `Nucleus Accumbens`, `Basillar artery` - 19 real rejected attempts) and found the dominant subclass is NOT a safety/detection gap.** `GroundingValidator`/`MCQValidator` already correctly reject every genuine instance - a formal P(x)-satisfaction analysis against the authoritative evidence (e.g. `P(x) = member of Corpus Striatum`: true for `Caudate Nucleus`/`Putamen`/`Nucleus Accumbens`, false for `Globus Pallidus`) confirms grounding's own free-text reasoning is already precisely correct every time this family fires. **This is a reliability problem - generation repeatedly attempts the same invalid question shape and exhausts the fixed 3-attempt budget - not a detection gap like WP-044/046/047 each closed.** The one candidate signal that had worked for a structurally similar prior problem (WP-046's `Corticospinal Tract` distractor-set differencing) was directly tested against real data and **falsified**: the same four-entity candidate set was found in both a failing and a successful `Caudate Nucleus` round - the deciding factor is the generated question's own semantic framing (bare category membership vs. identity-by-name or a target-unique property), which this architecture correctly declines to parse. **Outcome C** (no safe deterministic mechanism) for this subclass, reached only after directly testing and falsifying the one available candidate. **`MULTIPLE_SUBCLASSES`, not one problem**: `Basillar artery`'s own repeated rejections were found to be a separate, only-partially-understood shape - the evidence's own explicit `מקור:` labeling supports only `Basillar artery` as `Superior cerebellar artery`'s source, yet grounding repeatedly also credits `AICA` (a sibling branch sharing the same upstream parent) - recorded as a disclosed inference, not investigated further (out of scope, would need its own dedicated live-diagnostic investigation). **No production code was changed; no new pilot was run (not required, since no code changed) - this WP's entire investigation was built from real, already-captured production data.** **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: do not attempt a deterministic detection mechanism for this family - instead consider whether generation's own self-check against the existing "testing enumeration or classification targets" prompt guidance can be made more reliable, and investigate `Basillar artery`'s possible grounding-validator interpretation inconsistency separately, with fresh live diagnostic capture.** See `implementation/WP-048_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full P(x) analysis, classification table, and the specific reasons each candidate signal was rejected.

* **WP-047 searched this project's entire real historical dataset (54 real accepted pilot-category questions, WP-036 through WP-046) for a legitimate case where a named-entity target's correct answer intentionally identified a different entity, and found zero.** Every one of 7 real historical `answer != target` instances found (a downstream-entity substitution, three functional descriptions, an unrelated sibling entity, and the `Corticospinal Tract`/`Precentral Gyrus` shape twice) was already independently diagnosed, at the time, as a genuine defect - never a legitimate relationship-driven exception. **`_validate_target_answer_identity()` (new, `generation/generator.py`), implemented only after checking all eleven of WP-047's own explicit safety criteria, generalizes WP-044 Part B's narrow `is_source_role` pattern to every named-entity target**: rejects a candidate whose correct answer does not contain the target's own topic text (normalized, one-directional containment only - the answer must contain the target's name, never the reverse, to avoid accepting a bare partial-word fragment as a complete answer). **Validated safe specifically because WP-041's English-first policy already eliminates the legitimate-cross-script-answer scenario this exact check would otherwise have unsafely flagged** - every historical Hebrew-transliteration accepted answer in the dataset predates WP-041; `ConceptIdentity` was investigated and found insufficient for this purpose (never recognizes a cross-script form in this corpus) and was not used. **Live pilot: the new check fired zero times (directly verified per-attempt), yet all 7 accepted answers were independently confirmed target-identity-aligned (7/7 = 100%) - the first pilot in this project's history with a structural guarantee behind that number.** Raw acceptance fell to 7/12 (58.3%, down from WP-046's 11/12) - verifiably **not** attributable to this WP's own change: directly traced instead to WP-046's own distractor-containment check (fired 5 times, up from 3) and ordinary validator-level stochastic variance for two previously-100%-reliable targets (`Basillar artery`, `Nucleus Accumbens`). **A new, disclosed finding**: `Nucleus Accumbens`'s own rejections showed the identical generic classification-ambiguity shape already found unresolved for `Globus Pallidus`/`Caudate Nucleus` - a third real instance of that family - plus a related new shape observed for `Basillar artery` for the first time; neither addressed by this WP, per its own explicit scope boundary. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: investigate the generic classification-ambiguity family directly, now with a third real instance and a new candidate case, using the same evidence-first discipline as WP-044/045/046.** See `implementation/WP-047_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full target-to-answer matrix, relationship inventory, and safety analysis.

* **WP-046 built a deliberate case set from real production data across all three pilot categories (known failures, 22 real successful controls, confirmed parent/child cases, the mandatory `PICA` false-positive control) and, only after checking all ten of its own explicit safety criteria, implemented a narrower mechanism than WP-045 considered: `_validate_distractor_containment()` (new, `generation/generator.py`) rejects a candidate whose correct answer and any distractor stand in a textual containment relationship, for named-entity targets only.** This is scoped to the *actual, final, chosen* answer choices - never the broader category inventory WP-045's own rejected `has_named_child` signal used - which is exactly why it avoids that signal's confirmed false positive: `PICA`'s own actual generated distractors never include the confusable duplicate entity, so the check correctly never fires for it, live-confirmed in this WP's own pilot. **Live pilot: the check fired exactly 3 times, all for `Corticospinal Tract` round 3, each correctly blocking a real chosen distractor (`Lateral`/`Anterior Corticospinal Tract`) that contains the correct answer's own text - before any of the five validators even ran.** Zero false positives anywhere else across 12 rounds/17 attempts. **A new, important, honestly-disclosed finding: closing this one ambiguity pathway did not make `Corticospinal Tract` a fully safe target overall.** Round 4 was accepted, but its correct answer was `"Precentral Gyrus"` (the tract's own origin location) - not `"Corticospinal Tract"` itself - a live, independent recurrence of the exact target-identity weakness WP-043's architecture review first flagged; the new check correctly does not fire here, since this is a structurally different defect. Target alignment for this pilot: 10/11 (90.9%), for this specific, newly-disclosed reason, not smoothed over by the pilot's own 11/12 headline acceptance number. **A second new finding revised WP-045's own characterization**: `Caudate Nucleus` - previously cited as "reliably successful" - itself failed twice in WP-045's own pilot data with the identical generic classification-ambiguity framing that plagues `Globus Pallidus`, confirming this risk family is target-independent, not `Globus Pallidus`-specific; no safe deterministic signal was found for it, and `Globus Pallidus` remains explicitly, deliberately unresolved. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: investigate `Corticospinal Tract`'s newly-observed target-identity substitution directly** (consider whether WP-044 Part B's deterministic consistency-check pattern, currently scoped only to `is_source_role` targets, could be safely generalized to check the correct answer against the target's own topic for every named-entity target - with its own full, dedicated false-positive investigation, not assumed safe by analogy) **and separately investigate `Globus Pallidus`/`Caudate Nucleus`'s shared classification-ambiguity family as its own, still-unresolved problem.** See `implementation/WP-046_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full case set, the generalization matrix, and per-attempt validator reasoning.

* **WP-045 (diagnostic only, no production code) investigated `Globus Pallidus`/`Corticospinal Tract` in depth and found they do NOT share a root cause - `Corticospinal Tract`'s failure is a confirmed parent/child hierarchy ambiguity (every rejection explicitly named a named child as also evidence-supported); `Globus Pallidus`'s dominant failure mode is a broader classification ambiguity with no evidence-shape property distinguishing it from its reliably-successful siblings.** A candidate deterministic signal (`has_named_child` - another inventory concept's text contains the target's own text as a substring) was designed and tested directly against the real corpus, and rejected after finding a **confirmed false positive**: it also flags `Inferior Cerebellar Artery (PICA)`, a target that was cleanly accepted in both WP-044's and WP-045's own pilots - investigation found `"Posterior inferior cerebellar artery (PICA)"`/`"Inferior Cerebellar Artery (PICA)"` are the *same* real artery, extracted twice because one raw line is simply missing the word "Posterior," not a genuine hierarchy. Three narrowing refinements were each found to either miss the true positive or fail to exclude the false positive - true and false positives use the identical directional-adjective word class, so no safe vocabulary split exists. **A second, independent finding from WP-045's own fresh live pilot**: the same, unmodified production code produced BOTH a rejection and an acceptance for `Corticospinal Tract` across two different rounds of the same run, because whether the ambiguity surfaces as a rejection depends on the generator's own stochastic distractor choice for that specific attempt, not solely on the target's evidence shape - a second, independent reason a pre-generation evidence-shape-only skip is unsafe (it would have blocked the genuinely-successful attempt too). **No production code was changed.** The pilot's own higher acceptance number (11/12, up from WP-044's 8/12) is explicitly disclosed as unattributable stochastic variance, not a WP-045 fix - and came with a real, disclosed cost the raw number would otherwise hide: one accepted `Globus Pallidus` answer was a Hebrew functional description, not the target's own name, a live instance of the already-known WP-040/043 target-identity weakness. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: WP-045's own recommended direction is a post-generation, distractor-text-containment check (does a candidate distractor's own text contain, or get contained by, the correct answer's own text) targeted specifically at the confirmed `Corticospinal Tract` mechanism - narrower and more targeted than any pre-generation evidence-shape prediction, which this WP found unsafe on two independent grounds.** `Globus Pallidus`'s own dominant failure mode remains genuinely unexplained at the deterministic-signal level and should not be assumed solved by whatever fixes `Corticospinal Tract`. See `implementation/WP-045_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full diagnostic data, the three rejected refinement attempts, and per-attempt validator reasoning.

* **WP-044 extended the existing blueprint (never a new abstraction) with two structural generation constraints, and the live pilot showed a genuine, material acceptance recovery: 8/12 (66.7%), up from WP-043's 5/12 (41.7%) - meeting WP-044's own acceptance criterion without relaxing any validator or increasing the retry budget.** Part A (`is_enumeration_evidence_insufficient()`, `planning/concept_anchor.py`) detects the exact `Corpos Striatum` enumeration-intro-only shape WP-043 diagnosed (a narrow cue-phrase-in-backward-context check plus a near-empty-forward-context check, reusing the already-anchored evidence, no new retrieval) and skips such a concept entirely rather than build a target that could only produce a known-ambiguous generic membership question - confirmed both live (never selected in any of the four `גרעיני הבסיס` rounds, a complete reversal of WP-043's 4/4-selected-0/4-accepted result) and independently offline against today's real corpus. Part B (`extract_source_relationship_entity()`, `planning/target_role.py`) deterministically names the *specific* downstream entity a source-role target's evidence positions it as the source of (verified against the real corpus: `Basillar artery` → `"Superior cerebellar artery"`), surfaced into the existing evidence-role prompt section and backed by a new deterministic pre-validator check (`_validate_target_role_consistency()`, `generation/generator.py` - rejects a candidate whose correct answer names the downstream entity instead of the target; never an LLM validator, never a new entry in the five-validator pipeline) - confirmed live: `Basillar artery` was accepted on its second attempt, both attempts correctly framed as source/origin questions, never once the previously-dominant "which artery supplies X" downstream framing. **A new, more specific, honestly-disclosed finding**: the same underlying "generic classification/membership ambiguity" failure shape recurred for `Globus Pallidus` and `Corticospinal Tract` - both exhausted all 3 attempts, every rejection citing multiple equally-correct answers to an overly-generic question - but neither target's own narrow anchored evidence happens to contain an enumeration cue phrase within Part A's bounded detection window, so it correctly did not fire for them; this is not a defect in Part A (which behaves exactly as designed and tested) but evidence the underlying classification-ambiguity problem is broader than one narrow, deterministic signal can catch. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: does the same enumeration/classification-ambiguity failure shape need a broader (but still deterministic, still narrow, still explainable) detection signal, or does it need a different kind of structural constraint entirely** - `Globus Pallidus`/`Corticospinal Tract` are the next WP's own concrete, real, already-diagnosed starting examples, exactly the same "start from a demonstrated real failure, not a hypothetical" discipline this WP itself followed. See `implementation/WP-044_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round/per-attempt data (every validator's pass/fail and reason) and comparison against WP-036 through WP-043.

* **WP-043 built two deterministic, individually-correct, thoroughly-tested mechanisms addressing WP-042's own diagnosis - a root-cause anchoring fix for `Corpos Striatum`'s empty evidence, and a source-role detector for `Basillar artery`'s evidence shape - and the live pilot's overall acceptance regressed even further (5/12, down from WP-041's 9/12), not improved.** The anchoring fix is genuinely correct and verified (`InventoryConcept.source_line_indices`, new field, fixes a real line-lookup gap for WP-039 trailing-reconstructed concepts that previously made `anchor_concept_evidence()` silently fall back to a bare concept name; `is_factual_focus_sufficient()`/`anchor_concept_evidence(..., broad=True)` add a conservative, paragraph-boundary-respecting sufficiency check and fallback). The source-role detector (`planning/target_role.py`'s `detect_source_evidence_role()`, `QuestionTarget.is_source_role`, `format_target_evidence_role()`) is also genuinely correct (100% accurate on the real corpus). **But a dedicated post-pilot diagnostic capture (mirroring WP-042's own methodology) found two new, more specific failure modes**: `Corpos Striatum`'s newly-recovered context turned out to be an enumeration-introduction sentence ("basal nuclei contain several sub-structures"), which - combined with WP-040's mandatory single-entity answer requirement - appears to actively encourage an ambiguous list-membership question shape that grounding correctly and consistently rejects; `Basillar artery`'s evidence-role note was found not to reliably change generation's actual question-construction behavior (it kept asking "which artery supplies X" despite the suggested alternate framing), leaving acceptance dependent on stochastic grounding-validator interpretation of the transitive relationship - a supplementary capture showed the *same* evidence CAN occasionally succeed. **Neither finding indicates the underlying architectural approach is wrong** - both mechanisms correctly recovered genuine evidence/relationship information; surfacing that information turned out not to be sufficient, by itself, to make generation construct a compliant question. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: fixing these two newly-discovered, narrower problems (enumeration-shaped recovered context; unreliable adoption of the source-role framing) specifically - not reverting WP-043's own primitives, and not building a broader mechanism.** See `implementation/WP-042_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` and `implementation/WP-043_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full diagnostic data, per-attempt validator reasoning, and comparison tables.

* **WP-041 enforced a hard, deterministic English-first language policy (application decides, never the LLM) and it decisively fixed cross-script concept rotation live - but at a real, disclosed cost to acceptance that does not meet the WP's own stated success bar.** `format_target_language_requirement()` (new, `prompts/formatting.py`) states `TARGET LANGUAGE = English` whenever a named target's own topic is already English-representable (true for every real pilot-category target, by construction - no new model field, no bilingual-pairing search, no new validator) and requires the target's own English text verbatim for the correct answer and any in-question reference to it; the pre-existing base "must be written in Hebrew" instruction was given a precise, narrow carve-out (exactly the correct answer and target-name references, nothing else) rather than weakened generally. **Live pilot: English-first compliance was 9/9 (100%) and manual alignment was 9/9 (100%)** - the exact two concepts WP-040's own pilot had answered in Hebrew (`Corpos Striatum`, `Superior cerebellar artery`) both answered in English this run, confirmed independently offline first. **Concept rotation genuinely improved live for both previously cross-script-stuck categories** (`אספקת דם` 3/4 distinct, up from WP-040's 2/4; `גרעיני הבסיס` 2/4 distinct, up from WP-040's 1/4 stuck-every-round) - direct, live confirmation that an English answer is recognized by the existing, completely unmodified WP-034/036 exact-match coverage mechanism, with zero coverage-layer changes of any kind. **However, acceptance fell to 9/12 (75%), down from WP-040's 11/12 (91.7%)** - three ordinary `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError` failures (two for `Corpos Striatum` specifically), plausibly but not confirmedly attributable to a sixth simultaneous generation constraint now stacked on top of five pre-existing ones. This does **not** meet WP-041's own secondary success criterion ("do not materially degrade the WP-040 baseline") - reported honestly as a genuine, mixed result, not reframed as a clean win. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: is the acceptance cost worth the rotation/language-compliance gain, and if not, is there a way to recover reliability** (e.g. investigating whether `Corpos Striatum`'s own evidence quality, not the language requirement itself, is the deeper cause, before concluding the language policy itself needs revisiting) **before considering any expansion beyond the three-category pilot.** See `implementation/WP-041_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data, offline evaluation detail, and comparison against WP-036 through WP-040.

* **WP-040 made generation itself reliably answer with the assigned named-entity target, and manual alignment reached 11/11 (100%) among the live pilot's accepted questions - the misalignment pattern every prior pilot WP repeatedly observed did not recur once.** `QuestionTarget.named_entity_target` (new field, default `False`, set `True` only by the pilot-category deterministic planning path - every concept reaching that path is a named entity by construction, no new classifier introduced) and `format_target_answer_requirement()` (new, `prompts/formatting.py`) together add one new prompt section requiring a named-entity target's correct answer to identify the target itself, explicitly prohibiting the exact substitution shapes prior WPs' live pilots actually observed (functional description, property, related/sibling structure, neighboring entity, broader system) - never a language requirement, never a new validator/retry loop, per the WP-039 review's own explicit warning against recreating the reliability problem this project has repeatedly avoided. Offline evaluation (4 real generation calls on the concepts prior WPs' pilots actually struggled with) and the live pilot (11/12 accepted, matching WP-036/039's own baseline) both independently confirmed the fix: zero functional/property/related-entity answers anywhere in either evaluation. **This also cleanly isolated the one remaining blocker, exactly as the WP-039 review predicted it would**: `גרעיני הבסיס` still rotated to only 1/4 distinct concepts in the live pilot - not because generation failed to identify the target (it succeeded all four rounds, every answer a genuine Hebrew transliteration of the assigned English concept) but because WP-038's already-disclosed, deliberately-untouched cross-script coverage gap still cannot recognize a correct Hebrew answer against an English-stored concept. `מסילות עצביות` (whose answers happened to stay in English this run) rotated through 4/4 distinct concepts - its best result across every pilot WP to date. **One disclosed secondary cost**: average attempts-per-accepted-question rose from WP-039's ~1.09 to ~1.45, driven specifically by `גרעיני הבסיס` (avg 2.25) - still well within the existing 3-attempt budget, honestly reported rather than omitted. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: now that generation reliably identifies the target, the cross-script coverage gap WP-038 already investigated and declined to solve (Outcome C) is the single cleanly-isolated remaining blocker to full rotation for `אספקת דם`/`גרעיני הבסיס`** - WP-038's own investigation found no safe evidence-derived cross-script identity for these specific concepts, so revisiting this would need either a genuinely new angle (not transliteration/fuzzy matching, both already ruled out) or a deliberate decision to accept the current rotation ceiling for these two categories. See `implementation/WP-040_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data, offline evaluation detail, and comparison against WP-036 through WP-039.

* **WP-039 fixed trailing-fragment concept truncation at its correct architectural layer (extraction, not coverage), and its own primary success criterion is deterministically verified as met - but the live pilot's aggregate alignment number looks worse (~45%, down from WP-038's 80%) for reasons this WP explicitly does not touch, and that distinction matters for how the next WP should read this result.** New `_attempt_trailing_reconstruction()`/`_repair_trailing_truncations()` (`planning/concept_anchor.py`) detect and repair the same PDF bidi-extraction phenomenon WP-037's leading-truncation repair already targets, applied to a word's trailing end - e.g. `"Corpos Str"` → `"Corpos Striatum"`, `"Anterior Corticospinal T"` → `"...Tract"` - via a conservative walk that requires exactly one direction to yield continuation evidence, never guesses when ambiguous, and (a real bug found and fixed during development) marks fragment lines consumed even when a reconstruction attempt fails, preventing a *different*, unrelated concept from wrongly absorbing the same fragments. Offline analysis of all 3 pilot categories found 11 clean repairs, 2 correct exclusions, and exactly 1 disclosed residual limitation (`"Deep Brain Stimulatino D(SB.)"` - an intra-fragment OCR-level scramble, structurally indistinguishable from a valid fragment, explicitly out of scope). **Reproducing WP-038's own exact failure case offline confirms the fix works**: `CategoryCoverage(tested_concepts=("Anterior Corticospinal Tract",))` now correctly excludes the concept, where it did not before. The live pilot (11/12 accepted, matching WP-036's own baseline, up from WP-037's 8/12 and WP-038's 10/12) directly shows the fix operating in production - `גרעיני הבסיס`/`מסילות עצביות` now assign clean, complete concept names as targets instead of the truncated forms every prior WP was stuck showing. **However, manual target alignment fell to ~45% (5/11)** - traced honestly, category by category, to causes independent of this WP: ordinary generation drift in `אספקת דם` (a category WP-039 made *zero* repairs in) and a persistent functional-description-instead-of-structure-name answering pattern for `Corpos Striatum` that WP-037's own run already showed for the same concept before it was even completed. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: WP-037's/WP-038's own recommendation - reconsidering whether generation's answer language/framing should be constrained for pilot-category questions - remains the most promising unaddressed direction**, since extraction quality (this WP) and coverage-identity robustness (WP-038) are both now about as good as they can get without crossing into semantic/fuzzy matching or prompt redesign, neither of which this project's architecture reviews have sanctioned yet. See `implementation/WP-039_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data, offline per-concept table, and comparison against WP-036/037/038.
* **WP-038 introduced deterministic `ConceptIdentity`/`concept_identity_matches_text()` (`planning/concept_identity.py`) to make coverage recognition robust to language/script variation, and found - honestly, via a real pre-implementation investigation plus a live pilot - that safe, evidence-derived cross-script identity is not feasible for the concepts this pilot actually encounters (Outcome C).** The mechanism is genuinely implemented (deterministic normalization - whitespace/case/punctuation/Unicode-NFKC; plus a narrow, structural, evidence-derived alternate-language-form extractor requiring an explicit adjacent parenthetical pairing) and unit-tested working correctly when evidence supports it, but the actual pilot corpus was checked directly (not guessed at) and contains no such pairing for any of WP-037's live-pilot concepts - confirmed twice, once statically before implementation and once live across 12 pilot rounds (`concept_explicitly_supported_language_forms` was empty every time). General transliteration matching was deliberately not attempted: the live data shows the LLM's own Hebrew rendering of the same concept is not even self-consistent (three different renderings of "Superior cerebellar artery" across four rounds in this run alone), and building a phonetic matcher would itself be a fuzzy-matching mechanism, explicitly prohibited. Accepted count recovered partially (10/12, vs. WP-037's 8/12 and WP-036's 11/12) and manual alignment stayed high (8/10 = 80%, preserving WP-037's improvement, since anchoring itself was untouched), but `אספקת דם`/`גרעיני הבסיס` still reproduced WP-037's exact stuck-concept regression. **A second, distinct coverage-recognition failure mode was also surfaced live**: a trailing-truncated concept ("Anterior Corticospinal T") was correctly and completely answered ("...Tract"), but not recognized as covered because the *assigned* concept's own stored text is itself incomplete - a same-script completeness mismatch, not a language mismatch, but the identical downstream symptom; this is a direct second-order consequence of WP-037's own already-disclosed, still-unaddressed "trailing truncation is not detected" gap. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: per WP-037's own review and WP-038's own section 23, the recommended direction is now to reconsider whether generation's answer language should be constrained for pilot-category questions, rather than attempting further coverage-matching cleverness** - a prompt-contract change, out of both WP-037's and WP-038's explicit scope, and squarely the next WP's decision to make. See `implementation/WP-038_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data, safety analysis, and comparison tables.
* **WP-037 narrowed concept-anchored evidence for the same three pilot categories and found the anchoring fix worked, but surfaced a new, different problem: a Hebrew/English answer-language inconsistency breaking WP-034's exact-text coverage matching.** `refine_concept_inventory()`/`anchor_concept_evidence()` (new, `planning/concept_anchor.py`, both zero-LLM/zero-embedding) exclude category-self-restatement concepts (Hebrew "also called" cue-phrase proximity) and unambiguously-reconstructible leading-truncated concepts, then build a narrow, line-bounded evidence window around the selected concept instead of WP-036's wide fixed-character one. **Manually reviewing all 8 accepted questions for whether the actual answer semantically matches the assigned concept: 7/8 (87.5%) were aligned - a real improvement over WP-036's own ~45% manual rate** - confirming narrower anchoring does make generation more likely to test the assigned concept. **But a naive automated text-exact check would have shown only 2/8 (25%) "aligned"**, because generation often answered in Hebrew (transliterating the English concept name) rather than reusing it verbatim - and since WP-034's coverage mechanism does exact text matching, a concept correctly tested in Hebrew is never recognized as tested, so it gets reselected next round, producing near-duplicate questions that the unmodified WP-014 duplicate-replacement mechanism then correctly exhausts. Accepted count for the three categories fell from 11/12 (WP-036) to 8/12 (WP-037) as a direct, honestly-reported consequence. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: how should coverage matching account for language/script variation between an English-derived target concept and a possibly-Hebrew accepted answer** - without introducing synonym/semantic matching, which both WP-034 and WP-036/037 have deliberately deferred. See `implementation/WP-037_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data and manual alignment methodology.
* **WP-036 piloted deterministic, concept-inventory-constrained target planning for three categories (`אספקת דם`, `מסילות עצביות`, `גרעיני הבסיס`) and found a materially more specific negative result than WP-034's.** `extract_concept_inventory()` (new, `planning/concept_inventory.py`, pure/deterministic, zero LLM/embedding calls) extracts named concepts from retrieved evidence via structure-first, marker-based, honest-fallback heuristics (WP-035's recommended strategy); for the three pilot categories, `QuestionTargetPlanner.plan_targets()` now makes **zero LLM calls**, instead constructing `QuestionTarget`s directly from whichever inventory concepts remain after excluding `coverage.tested_concepts` (WP-034, exact match only). **Target selection worked exactly as designed - a different concept was correctly assigned every round - but the diversity problem was not fixed**: generation repeatedly produced questions whose *actual* correct answer diverged from the *assigned* concept (drifting back to a more salient neighboring entity, or - for one category - to a broad category-level restatement), so the assigned concept was never marked tested and got reassigned again next round. **Root cause, evidence-grounded: coverage exclusion can only see what generation actually produces, and a target's `factual_focus` context window does not yet reliably anchor generation to test exactly that concept as the answer.** **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: fixing this requires either better-isolated context-window extraction (so a concept's supporting text doesn't include a more salient neighbor) or a prompt change that explicitly directs generation to test the assigned concept as the answer** - the latter is a prompt-redesign question this pilot's own scope explicitly excluded. Accepted counts were comparable to or better than each category's own WP-034 baseline (no reliability regression) - the failure is specific to diversity, not correctness. See `implementation/WP-036_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-round data and explicit non-expansion recommendation.
* **`extract_concept_inventory()`/`InventoryConcept`/`PILOT_CATEGORIES` are new, small, and confined to `planning/concept_inventory.py`** - no public contract changed, no prompt file was touched (pilot-category planning bypasses the planning prompt entirely rather than modifying it). `QuestionTargetPlanner.__init__` gained an optional `pilot_categories: frozenset[str]` parameter (defaulting to the real WP-036 set) purely for test isolation.
* **WP-035 (investigation only, no production code) concluded deterministic concept ownership is feasible for a subset of categories with clean, list-structured evidence, chosen from real-evidence study rather than speculation** - see `implementation/WP-035_ARCHITECTURE_INVESTIGATION.md` for the full candidate-strategy comparison (WP-036 implemented its top recommendation as a pilot).
* **WP-034 made target planning coverage-aware, and found - reported honestly, not reframed - that it did NOT meaningfully fix the WP-032 diversity regression.** `CategoryCoverage` (new, deliberately internal-only, `models/coverage.py`) and `extract_category_coverage()` (new, `planning/coverage.py`, pure/deterministic, zero LLM/embedding calls) summarize each category's already-generated questions' correct-answer text and inferred relationship types (reusing WP-030's own `classify_relationship_type()`, now factored out of `extract_relationship()` for reuse - no new classification scheme). This is threaded into `QuestionTargetPlanner.plan_targets()` (gained an optional `coverage` parameter, defaulted for full backward compatibility) and rendered into the target-planning prompt as one new "already-tested knowledge" section - information only, never a validator (section 6), never a second LLM call or retry (planning still makes exactly one call, per WP-025's own rule). **The live acceptance run's diversity review found only a marginal, statistically-insignificant improvement**: 7/15 (47%) category pairs DISTINCT vs. WP-032's 7/17 (41%) - and a controlled same-category evaluation (the exact category WP-033 used) showed **zero** improvement: all four sequential questions converged on the identical fact despite the coverage section correctly and verifiably flagging it as already-tested. **Explicit conclusion (WP-034 section 14, evidence-based not speculative): coverage is correctly computed and correctly delivered, but soft information-only prompt guidance does not reliably override the model's tendency to select the single most salient fact in a category's evidence, especially when that category has one clearly dominant fact and comparatively weaker alternatives.** **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: does making coverage-awareness actually work require constraining retrieval/evidence itself (excluding or de-weighting already-tested evidence before the planner ever sees it), rather than only informing an otherwise-unconstrained planning call?** That would mean touching retrieval, which WP-034 section 13 explicitly kept out of scope - a deliberate, disclosed boundary, not an oversight. See `implementation/WP-034_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full data and per-category diversity table.
* **`CategoryCoverage`/`extract_category_coverage()`/`classify_relationship_type()` are new, small, and free-standing** - no existing model gained a new field, and `CategoryQuestionSetRequest`/`Response` (WP-033) were **not** touched (WP-034 section 7 explicitly forbade adding new fields to that now-stable contract) - coverage is re-derived fresh from `existing_questions` on every call, never persisted or passed separately. Any future WP wanting a different coverage representation should extend `extract_category_coverage()`'s output, not the request contract.
* **WP-033 replaced WP-032's request/response contract with the permanent one: `CategoryQuestionSetRequest`/`Response` (`category_generation/models.py`), where `existing_questions` and the generated question both use `ExamQuestion` (WP-002's production schema, reused - never a duplicate model) instead of the internal `CandidateQuestion`.** `ExamOrchestrator` now constructs `CategoryQuestionSetRequest` and calls `CategoryQuestionSetService` - `CategoryGenerationRequest`/`CategoryGenerationService` (WP-032) remain fully present and functional (directly tested) but are no longer what the orchestrator uses, per WP-033 section 7's explicit backward-compatibility requirement. `ExamQuestion.number` became optional (`PositiveIntStrict | None = None`, `models/question.py`) - generation itself never assigns a runtime identifier (`CategoryQuestionSetService` always returns `number=None`); `ExamOrchestrator` still assigns the real number when building the final exam, unchanged. `schemas/exam_output.schema.json` changed accordingly (expected, disclosed - `number` no longer `required`); the other two schema files are unaffected. Both services now share one underlying implementation (`_run_generation_cycle()`) - no duplicated decision logic. **No diversity/planner/prompt/validator change of any kind** - the four-scenario architectural smoke test showed the same target-planning-granularity convergence WP-032 already found (not a new regression, not evaluated further per WP-033's own scope). See `implementation/WP-033_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s "Category Question Set Contract" section for the full rationale.
* **WP-032 replaced the project's primary generation API - "Generate Exam" is no longer the top-level capability; "Generate Next Question For Category" is, and `ExamOrchestrator` is now just a client loop over it.** (Its own request/response contract, `CategoryGenerationRequest`/`Service`, has since been superseded as the orchestrator's own path by WP-033 above - `CategoryGenerationService` itself is unchanged and still works.) This was a pure architectural refactoring (section 13 explicitly excludes prompt/validator/retrieval/relationship/competitor/diversity changes) - the request/response contracts, service responsibilities, and full V1-vs-V2 rationale are in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s "V1 vs. V2 Generation Architecture" section. **However, the live acceptance run (run exclusively through the new architecture, never through `ExamOrchestrator`/the CLI) surfaced a real, honestly-reported side effect: diversity regressed sharply (only 7/17 category pairs DISTINCT, vs. WP-031's 12/12) and efficiency worsened (avg 1.58 attempts/accepted vs. WP-031's 1.16; grounding "another answer also supported" rose to 20 from 16), even though the total accepted count improved (36/40 vs. 32/40).** The leading hypothesis: target planning narrowed from "once per category, `count=N`, mutually co-aware" (V1) to "once per `generate_next()` call, `count=1`, blind to sibling targets already used for the same category" (V2) - an inherent structural consequence of the new per-question API shape, not a deliberate diversity change (explicitly out of scope for WP-032, section 13). **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: how should a future WP restore category-level target co-awareness within the new per-question API** - e.g. by having the caller pass already-used target descriptions alongside `existing_questions`, or by giving the service some other way to avoid re-covering ground already tested for the same category - without regressing the clean, minimal per-question contract WP-032/WP-033 established. WP-032's own architecture review reached the same conclusion independently: the next planner should become "coverage-aware," reasoning about previously-tested concepts/relationships from `existing_questions` rather than returning to batch planning. See `implementation/WP-032_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full data, including the per-category diversity/false-acceptance tables.
* **`CategoryGenerationRequest`/`CategoryGenerationResponse` (`category_generation/models.py`) are WP-032's original request/response contracts, kept working for backward compatibility but no longer the orchestrator's own path (see the WP-033 bullet above)** - `category`/`generation_mode`/`existing_questions`/`generation_options` in, `accepted`/`production`/`duplicate_replacement_attempts`/`failure_type`/`failure_message` out (`question`/`attempts` are derived `@property` accessors, not stored fields). A future WP extending the generation API surface should build on `CategoryQuestionSetRequest`/`Response` instead. `ExamOrchestrator.__init__` takes only `category_resolver` and `category_question_set_service` - not `target_planner`/`producer`/`max_duplicate_replacement_attempts` directly; those three live on `CategoryQuestionSetService.__init__` (and `CategoryGenerationService.__init__` identically), both raising `InvalidCategoryGenerationConfigurationError` (replacing the no-longer-existing `InvalidOrchestrationConfigurationError`).

* **WP-031 (Phase 2 of the WP-029 migration) showed real, partial progress on the metric WP-030 failed to move.** Grounding rejections caused by "another answer also supported" fell from WP-030's 24 to 16 - a genuine improvement, though still above WP-027's original 12. Average attempts per accepted question (1.16) and first-attempt acceptance rate (28/32) both reached the best level of any run measured so far. False-acceptance human review found only 1 `POSSIBLE_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER` among 32 accepted (0 `CONFIRMED`) - tying WP-027 as the cleanest correctness result yet. **However, an offline investigation found the improvement is not cleanly attributable to competitor discovery alone**: among the 8 planned positions with a grounding failure, the proportion that were relationship-classified (and thus eligible for competitor discovery) was statistically similar to the overall classified/unclassified split - a real but not fully explained improvement. Relationship-keyword coverage remains the binding constraint (only 15/40 targets classified this run) - the recurring PNS-divisions target still fails, still `UNSPECIFIED`, still outside the keyword table's coverage (uses "מחולק ל," not yet in the table). See `implementation/WP-031_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full data and the explicit "did this improve quality" evaluation.
* **`CompetitorCandidate` (new, `models/competitor.py`) and `discover_competitors()` (new, `generation/competitors.py`)** - a small immutable model plus a pure, deterministic, zero-LLM-call function that scans already-retrieved evidence (excluding the target's own supporting chunks) for other passages sharing the same relationship keyword, threaded into every generation call via a new required `GenerationPromptContext.competitors` field. Neither `QuestionTarget` nor `QuestionRelationship` gained any new field. Any future WP touching generation's prompt-context construction must account for this new required parameter, in addition to WP-030's `relationship` field.

* **WP-030 (Phase 1 of the WP-029 migration) did NOT meet its own primary success metric.** Grounding rejections caused by "another answer also supported" rose to 24 in the full acceptance run (vs. WP-027's 12 and WP-028's 14) - worse than either prior WP on this exact metric, despite a clean 6/6 focused-evaluation result beforehand. **However, MCQ rejections (9, back to WP-027-like levels, down from WP-028's 18) and quality rejections (3, better than both WP-027's 10 and WP-028's 15) improved substantially** - real evidence the relationship-type framing helped structural/wording clarity even though it did not address the dominant grounding failure class. Only 17/40 planned targets (42.5%) actually matched a known relationship keyword this run, and the relationship-classified targets did not fail at a meaningfully higher rate than unclassified ones - the grounding regression is not clearly attributable to the relationship layer itself. Correctness held (0 `CONFIRMED_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER`), but one accepted question directly reproduced the original WP-025A/026 enumeration-ambiguity shape (a target's own text grouped three sibling events under one shared relationship; only one was designated correct) - flagged as `POSSIBLE_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER`. **The most important open question for whoever picks up next: given this result, should WP-029's Phase 2 (evidence-anchored distractor construction) proceed as originally scoped, or does the grounding regression warrant fresh diagnostic work first?** See `implementation/WP-030_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full data.
* **`QuestionRelationship` (new, `models/relationship.py`) and `extract_relationship()` (new, `generation/relationship.py`)** - a small immutable model plus a pure, deterministic, zero-LLM-call keyword classifier threaded into every generation call via a new required `GenerationPromptContext.relationship` field. `QuestionTarget` itself gained no new field. Any future WP touching generation's prompt-context construction must account for this new required parameter.

* **WP-028's live full acceptance run did NOT improve on WP-027 by the metric WP-028 itself set out to move: 31/40 accepted (vs. 34/40), and grounding rejections caused by "another answer also supported" - the WP's own stated primary success metric - rose from 12 to 14 rather than falling.** MCQ/quality/category rejections all rose too. **Correctness held at the same bar** (0 `CONFIRMED_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER` among 31 accepted questions, one more `POSSIBLE` case than WP-027's run - both single-run results). The leading documented hypothesis: the blueprint's `evidence_checked` and other fields are the model's own **self-reported**, never independently-verified claims, unlike WP-027's per-option grounding assessment (checked by a genuinely separate validator call) - the same "never trust the generator's own claim" principle applied everywhere else in this architecture may apply to WP-028's mechanism itself. No prompt tuning was performed after seeing this result, per the WP's own explicit instruction. **A future WP revisiting generation-quality improvement should treat this as evidence that self-reported internal reasoning inside a single call is not a reliable substitute for independent verification by a separate call** - see `implementation/WP-028_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full data and reasoning before deciding whether to extend, revert, or replace the blueprint mechanism.
* **`GeneratedQuestionResponse` (LLM-facing) gained a required `blueprint: QuestionBlueprint` field** (`models/question.py`) - `knowledge_target`/`tested_relationship`/`question_style`/`intended_difficulty`/`correct_answer_role`/`distractors` (exactly 3 `DistractorDesign`, each with an `archetype`, `plausibility_reason`, `incorrectness_reason`, `evidence_checked`). The public `CandidateQuestion` contract is **completely unchanged** - the blueprint is discarded immediately after use, never persisted, never validated independently. Any future WP touching generation's LLM-facing contract must account for this new required field.
* **WP-027's live 40-question acceptance run (20 categories x 2 questions, real OpenAI API) was `PARTIAL` - 34/40 accepted, 6 failed, all normal `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError`.** The false-acceptance human review of all 34 accepted questions found **0 `CONFIRMED_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER` and only 1 `POSSIBLE_SECOND_CORRECT_ANSWER`** (a genuine real-world domain subtlety, not a validator failure) - meeting WP-027's primary quality goal. All 12 grounding-caused rejections in this run were specifically "another answer also supported," confirming the strengthened per-option check is actively catching the exact defect class WP-026 exposed, at scale, not just in the specifically-diagnosed cases. Diversity review: 14/14 DISTINCT among category pairs with both questions accepted - target-based diversity (WP-025) and target-aware framing (WP-026) both remain intact alongside the new grounding strength. See `implementation/WP-027_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full analysis.
* **`GroundingValidationResponse` (LLM-facing) no longer carries `correct_answer_supported`/`other_answers_not_equally_correct`/top-level `evidence_refs` - it carries `answer_assessments: list[GroundingAnswerAssessment]` instead** (one per answer index 1-4, each with its own `supported_as_correct`/`evidence_refs`/`reason`). The public `GroundingValidationResult` contract is completely unchanged - both summary booleans are now deterministically derived in `_resolve_grounding_response()` (`validation/grounding.py`), never LLM-reported. Any future WP touching grounding's LLM-facing contract must account for this - it is not the same shape WP-022 originally introduced.
* **`MCQValidator`/`QualityValidator` remain deliberately evidence-free** - WP-027 explicitly kept factual-correctness responsibility solely in `GroundingValidator`, per its own diagnostic's architectural analysis. A future WP should not casually add evidence access to either without revisiting that analysis (`evaluation/wp026_false_acceptance_diagnostic.md`).
* **Retrieval-query construction (`_build_validation_query()`) was investigated but deliberately left unchanged** - offline analysis of all 4 originally-diagnosed cases (#8/#14/#23/#32) showed the needed evidence was already being retrieved in every case; the failure was in reasoning over already-adequate evidence, not evidence insufficiency. A future WP revisiting this should bring fresh evidence, not assume the decision is permanent.
* **WP-026 was a prompt-only change; WP-027 added the `GroundingAnswerAssessment` model, deterministic derivation logic, and prompt changes to both grounding and generation** - no validator was added, no attempt-limit or retry-count change, no retrieval/TF-IDF/chunking change, no embeddings.
* **WP-025's live 40-question acceptance run (20 categories x 2 questions, real OpenAI API) was `PARTIAL` - 36/40 accepted, 4 failed, all normal `QuestionAttemptsExhaustedError` (MCQ/quality-validator rejections), zero generation-contract failures.** Target assignment itself introduced no new failure mode - the failures are the same pre-existing WP-013 mechanism, just slightly more frequent than WP-024's 0/40 (a narrower, target-focused question may be modestly harder to phrase with one unambiguous best answer than an unconstrained one - one run, not a large-sample conclusion). **Diversity review of all 16 category pairs with both questions accepted: 16/16 DISTINCT, 0 borderline, 0 duplicate** - a clear, live-verified improvement over WP-024's exam (6 near-duplicate/duplicate pairs out of 20). See `implementation/WP-025_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` for the full per-category table.
* **Generation and target-planning both now require a `target: QuestionTarget` parameter** - `QuestionGenerator.generate_candidate_question()`, `QuestionProducer.produce_question()`. `ExamOrchestrator` gained a required `target_planner: QuestionTargetPlanner` constructor dependency. Any future WP touching generation or orchestration construction must account for this - it is not optional/defaulted.
* **`QuestionTarget` carries no persistence into the audit** - deliberately mirrors the existing `CandidateQuestion`-carries-no-evidence-field precedent (WP-009/WP-015). If a future WP wants planned-target visibility in `exam_audit.json` itself (rather than only via `QuestionTargetPlanner.plan_history`, an in-process observability property), that is a new, explicit decision to make - it was deliberately not done here to keep WP-025's footprint minimal.
* **The question-local/system-level classification gained exactly one new question-local case**: `InsufficientDistinctTargetsError` (a planned position with no corresponding target - never calls the producer, never consumes WP-013's attempt budget). Every other WP-023 classification is unchanged. Do not casually reclassify more failure types as question-local without checking whether an existing test already asserts system-level treatment for it.
* **No post-generation diversity validator exists** - a deliberate WP-025 decision (section 11: prefer the simpler design, add one only if target-identity-by-construction proves insufficient). The live evidence above supported not adding one; a future WP revisiting this should look at fresh evidence, not assume the decision is permanent.
* **`GeneratedQuestionResponse.evidence_chunk_ids` no longer exists - it is `evidence_refs: list[int]`** (WP-024, mirroring WP-022's grounding/textbook pattern exactly; WP-025's own planning response follows the identical pattern).
* **`ExamGenerationResult`/`ExamOutputBundle.exam` are `ExamOutput | None`** (WP-023) - `None` only in the all-planned-questions-failed edge case.
* **TF-IDF retrieval remains evidenced as sufficient for V1** - Recall@3=86.5%, Recall@5/@8=94.6%, unchanged since WP-017; no WP since has touched retrieval, confirmed again unchanged by WP-025.
* **Two audit-provenance gaps remain open, by deliberate WP-015 scope decision, still unchanged**: `QuestionAudit.historical_reference_id`/`.evidence` are always `None`/`[]`; prompt-version/hash provenance is not captured anywhere.
* **No historical-reference rotation or answer-position balancing exists** - unchanged, still out of scope, still deferred. (Exam-level *within-category* diversity is now handled by WP-025; cross-category diversity remains explicitly out of scope per WP-025 section 14.)

The environment's Python interpreter is 3.12.3, not 3.14 as WP-001.md assumed; see Known Issues above. `openpyxl>=3.1` (3.1.5), `pymupdf>=1.24` (1.28.0), `scikit-learn>=1.4` (1.9.0), and `openai>=2.0` (2.52.0) are the real runtime dependencies so far; no WP since WP-007 has added a new one.

Do not reconstruct implementation from memory or from another repository.

Do not copy implementation decisions that are not recorded in the approved architecture or explicitly specified by the Work Package.

**V1 is accepted (with known limitations); WP-023 through WP-053 are also complete (WP-029 design-only, WP-035 investigation-only, WP-042/WP-045/WP-048/WP-050/WP-051/WP-052 diagnostic-only, WP-053 a controlled experiment with no permanent production code).** No WP-054 has been supplied yet; the next implementation task awaits the architect/user's review of this WP-053 completion report before any further work is defined. Claude must not invent or begin a WP-054 specification.

## Required Update Template for Future WPs

After every successfully completed Work Package, update the relevant sections above and record:

### Current State

* Last completed WP
* Next planned WP
* Overall phase

### Implemented

Concise list of behavior actually implemented.

### Important Interfaces

New/changed public classes, functions, commands, schemas, configuration contracts, or file formats needed by later WPs.

### Tests

* Total
* Passing
* Failing
* Verification commands/results

### Decisions Made

Only decisions actually established during implementation.

### Known Issues

Current unresolved defects/risks.

### Files Added

Files created by the WP.

### Files Significantly Modified

Existing files materially changed.

### Deferred Work

Anything intentionally left for later WPs.

### Next WP Context

Facts the next WP author/implementer must know. Do not invent the next WP specification.
